large enough, with the bias and variance of the REP gradient estimator increasing and decreasing with
\alpha
, respectively. In addition, the highest the value of
\alpha
(and the lowest the value of
\epsilon
), the lowest
N
needs to be before the observed SNR and the prediction made by (
50
) coincide. This follows from the fact that the second-order term in the numerator of the SNR when
\alpha\in(0,1)
and
\epsilon>0
is expected to vanish quicker with
N
as
\alpha
increases and
\epsilon
decreases.

As
d
increases,
\mathrm{SNR}[\phi_{k}\text{-}g_{1,N}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)]
should revert to
\mathrm{SNR}[\phi_{k}\text{-}g_{1,1}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)]
according to
Example

3
unless the number of samples
N
is very large or
\epsilon
is small, meaning that the asymptotic behavior (
50
) will not reflect the observed SNRs anymore. This is indeed what we observe in Figure
1
(
N=10^{0}
corresponds to
\mathrm{SNR}[\phi_{k}\text{-}g_{1,1}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)]=\epsilon
), with the weight collapse occurring quicker the higher the value of
\epsilon
, that is the further the variational approximation is from the target posterior density. Interpreting the different values of
\epsilon
as different stages of the training procedure,
Example

3
sheds light on the role the flexibility of the variational family plays in the success of the REP gradient estimator: the advantages of using the REP gradient estimator with
N>1
instead of with
N=1
arise after an initial training period ensuring that the quality of the variational approximation increases.

Additional results for the DREP gradient estimator and for the IWAE case
(\alpha=0)
leading to similar conclusions are provided in
Section

E.1
for the sake of completeness.

4.2
Linear Gaussian example

We are next interested in the linear Gaussian example from
Example

2
. The data set
\mathcal{D}=\{x_{1},...,x_{T}\}
is generated by sampling
T=1024
datapoints from
\mathcal{N}(0,2\boldsymbol{I}_{d})
. We let
\theta=\theta^{\star}+2\epsilon
,
A=A^{\star}
and
\tilde{b}=\tilde{b}^{\star}+2\epsilon
with
\epsilon>0
,
\theta^{\star}=T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}x_{t}
,
A^{\star}=\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{I}_{d}
and
\tilde{b}^{\star}=\frac{1}{2}\theta^{\star}
, so that
Ax+\tilde{b}=\frac{1}{2}(x+\theta)+\epsilon
. For all
\alpha\in[0,1)
and all
k=1\ldots d
,
Example

2
thus states that
Theorems

3
and
4
predict the asymptotic behavior as
N\to\infty
of
\mathrm{SNR}[\tilde{b}_{k}\text{-}g_{1,N}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)]
and
\mathrm{SNR}[\tilde{b}_{k}\text{-}g_{1,N}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{DREP})}(\theta,\phi;x)]
respectively.

Let us now check the validity of the asymptotic results obtained in
Example

2
. Focusing first on the learning of
\tilde{b}
when
\alpha\in(0,1)
, this boils down to checking whether as
N
increases the observed
\mathrm{SNR}[\tilde{b}_{k}\text{-}g_{1,N}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)]
and
\mathrm{SNR}[\tilde{b}_{k}\text{-}g_{1,N}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{DREP})}(\theta,\phi;x)]
match with the asymptotic behaviors: as
N\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{SNR}[\tilde{b}_{k}\text{-}g_{1,N}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)]=\penalty\ \frac{\sqrt{N}\frac{3\epsilon\alpha}{4-\alpha}(1+o(1))}{\frac{(4-\alpha)^{d/2}}{(15-6\alpha)^{d/4}}\exp\left(\frac{12(1-\alpha)^{2}}{(4-\alpha)(5-2\alpha)}d\epsilon^{2}\right)\sqrt{{\frac{2}{5-2\alpha}+\left(\frac{12(1-\alpha)\epsilon}{(5-2\alpha)(4-\alpha)}\right)^{2}}}}

(51)

\displaystyle\mathrm{SNR}[\tilde{b}_{k}\text{-}g_{1,N}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{DREP})}(\theta,\phi;x)]={4}{\alpha}^{-1}\penalty\ \mathrm{SNR}[\tilde{b}_{k}\text{-}g_{1,N}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)]

(52)

(note that once more only the leading order term remains in (
51
)). Now let
\epsilon\in\{0.2,1\}
,
\alpha\in\{0.1,0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9\}
,
d\in\{10,100\}
,
N\in\{2^{j},j=1\ldots 14\}
and
\tilde{b}_{k}
be a random coordinate in
(\tilde{b}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{b}_{d})
. The results are shown in Figures
2
and
3
.

Figure 2:
Plotted is
\mathrm{SNR}[\tilde{b}_{k}\text{-}g_{1,N}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)]
computed over 2000 Monte Carlo samples for the Linear Gaussian example described
in
Section

4.2
as a function of
N
, for varying values of
(\alpha,d,\epsilon)
and a randomly selected
datapoint
x
. The solid lines correspond to
\mathrm{SNR}[\tilde{b}_{k}\text{-}g_{1,N}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)]
, the dashed lines correspond to predictions of the form (
51
).

Figure 3:
Plotted is
\mathrm{SNR}[\tilde{b}_{k}\text{-}g_{1,N}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{DREP})}(\theta,\phi;x)]
computed over 2000 Monte Carlo samples for the Linear Gaussian example described in
Section

4.2
as a function of
N
, for varying values of
(\alpha,d,\epsilon)
and a randomly selected
datapoint
x
. The solid lines correspond to
\mathrm{SNR}[\tilde{b}_{k}\text{-}g_{1,N}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{DREP})}(\theta,\phi;x)]
, the dashed lines correspond to predictions of the form (
51
).

Starting with Figure
2
, we see that the conclusions drawn in
Section

4.1
for the REP gradient estimator regarding the validity of the asymptotic behaviors predicted by
Theorems

3
and
4
when
\alpha\in(0,1)
apply here too. Moving on to Figure
3
, we have that these conclusions are also valid for the DREP gradient estimator, which notably confirms empirically that the DREP gradient estimator outperforms the REP one by a
4\alpha^{-1}
factor in terms of SNR for the setting considered here. Additional plots in which
we replace
\tilde{b}_{k}
by
\theta_{k}
and/or we set
\alpha=0
are available in
Section

E.2
. They further validate our conclusions regarding the empirical validity of
Theorems

3
and
4
.

As
d
and
\epsilon
increase,
\mathrm{SNR}[\tilde{b}_{k}\text{-}g_{1,N}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{DREP})}(\theta,\phi;x)]
reverts to
\mathrm{SNR}[\tilde{b}_{k}\text{-}g_{1,1}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{DREP})}(\theta,\phi;x)]
in Figure
2
unless
N
is increasingly large and we observe a similar behavior for the DREP gradient estimator in Figure
3
, which is coherent with our conjecture that an increasing mismatch between the posterior and variational densities takes precedence over the choice of the importance-weighted variational bound. This effect can to some extent be mitigated by increasing
\alpha
or
N
, although we argue that improving on the quality of the variational approximation through the learning of
\phi
is a more direct approach to fully leverage the advantages of importance-weighted VI gradient estimators.

4.3
Variational auto-encoder

We consider the case of a variational auto-encoder (VAE) model designed to generate MNIST digits with a
d
-dimensional latent space, where
p_{\theta}(z)
is a fixed standard Gaussian distribution,
p_{\theta}(x|z)
is a product over the output dimensions of independent Bernoulli random variables with logits
\pi_{\theta}(z)
,
q_{\phi}(z|x)=\mathcal{N}(z;\mu_{\phi}(x),\sigma_{\phi}(x))
and the functions
\pi_{\theta}(z)
and
(\mu_{\phi}(x),\sigma_{\phi}(x))
are parameterized by neural networks. More precisely, both the encoding and decoding networks are MLPs with two hidden layers of size 200 and
\tanh
nonlinearities.

Let us then check the validity of our asymptotic results for this more involved real-data example. Focusing on the case
\alpha\in(0,1)
,
Theorem

3
predicts that: as
N\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{SNR}[\psi\text{-}g_{1,N}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)]=\sqrt{N}\,\dfrac{\left|\partial_{\psi}\mathrm{VR}^{(\alpha)}(\theta,\phi;x)+o(1)\right|}{\sqrt{\psi\text{-}V^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)}+o\left(1\right)}.

(53)

Unlike the two previous examples, we do not have a closed form expression for
\partial_{\psi}\mathrm{VR}^{(\alpha)}(\theta,\phi;x)
and
\psi\text{-}V^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)
here and we will resort to using approximations instead. Letting
N^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}
, we approximate
\partial_{\psi}\mathrm{VR}^{(\alpha)}(\theta,\phi;x)
by
\psi\text{-}g_{1,N^{\prime}}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)
following
Theorem

2
and we approximate
\psi\text{-}V^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)
by

{N^{\prime}}\penalty\ \sum_{i=1}^{N^{\prime}}\left(\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon_{1,i};x)^{1-\alpha}}{\sum_{\ell=1}^{N^{\prime}}\penalty\ \tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon_{1,\ell};x)^{1-\alpha}}\left[\partial_{\psi}\left(\log\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon_{1,i};x)\right)-\psi\text{-}g_{1,N^{\prime}}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)\right]\right)^{2}

with
\varepsilon_{1,1},\ldots,\varepsilon_{1,N^{\prime}}
being i.i.d. samples generated from
\tilde{q}
. Letting
\alpha\in\{0.1,0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9\}
,
d\in\{10,100\}
,
N\in\{2^{j},j=1\ldots 10\}
,
N^{\prime}=2^{10}
, we then obtain Figure
4
in which
\psi\in\{\theta_{k},\phi_{k^{\prime}}\}
with
k
and
k^{\prime}
that were randomly selected.

Figure 4:
Plotted is
\mathrm{SNR}[\psi\text{-}g_{1,N}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)]
computed over 2000 Monte Carlo samples for the VAE example described in
Section

4.3
as a function of
N
, for varying values of
(\alpha,d)
,
\psi\in\{\theta_{k},\phi_{k^{\prime}}\}
and a randomly selected datapoint
x
. The solid lines correspond to
\mathrm{SNR}[\psi\text{-}g_{1,N}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)]
, the dashed lines correspond to predictions of the form (
53
) in which
\partial_{\psi}\mathrm{VR}^{(\alpha)}(\theta,\phi;x)
and
\psi\text{-}V^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)
have also been approximated.

Even though we had to resort to an additional layer of approximation here, our conclusions remain similar to the ones obtained in
Sections

4.1
and
4.2
, in the sense that the behavior predicted in (
53
) matches the observed rates for
N
sufficiently large and we can observe the impact of increasing the mismatch between the variational and posterior densities via
d
. The theoretical studies we have carried out in this paper thus provides a useful framework to understand importance-weighted VI methods in real-data scenarios.

5
Conclusion

We proposed two complementary SNR analyses which advance the understanding of importance-weighted VI methods by casting a new light on the innerworkings of the ELBO, IWAE, VR, and VR-IWAE bound methodologies at the gradient level.
Our proof techniques rely on novel theoretical tools that apply more broadly within importance weighting and are of independent interest. The validity of our analyses is demonstrated over several toy and real-data examples and we now mention three potential directions of research to extend our work.

A first direction of research, also outlined at the end of
Section

3.4
, is to investigate the extension of our results for the Gaussian random field case to the approximate Gaussian random field one in order to encompass more general settings of interest. Another possibility is to see how our results can be further exploited to tune
\alpha
and/or to choose the variational family. Finally, our work showcases that there is much to gain theoretically and empirically from investigating the connections between sampling and importance-weighted VI. One may seek to delve deeper into those connections with importance weighting and related Monte Carlo approximations.

Acknowledgements and Disclosure of Funding

We are grateful to the Associate Editor and to the reviewers for their insightful comments which helped us improve the manuscript. We also want to thank Pierre E. Jacob and El Mahdi Khribch for stimulating discussions that encouraged us to pursue weaker positive moment conditions for (
20
) in
Theorem

1
. These exchanges prompted a re-examination of our proof strategy, which ultimately allowed us to obtain the refined results presented in
Theorem

1
.

Appendix A
Interchanging Derivative and Expectation Signs

Throughout the paper, we often come across expectations of the form

\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(g(\psi,\varepsilon)\right)
for some real valued
parameter
\psi
in a parameter space
\Psi
,
which we want to differentiate w.r.t
\psi
. More specifically, using
that
\tilde{q}
does not depend on
\psi
, we want compute the derivative by
interchanging the derivative and the expectation signs, that is

\displaystyle\partial_{\psi}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(g(\psi,\varepsilon)\right)=\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\partial_{\psi}g(\psi,\varepsilon)\right),\quad\psi\in\Psi\;.

(54)

General conditions to make the identity (
54
) valid are
well known (see, e.g.,
L’Ecuyer, 1995
) and specifying them can be overlooked at first reading as it sometimes burdens the technical
content with lengthy assumptions whose sole aim is to make interchanges
of derivatives and expectations well justified. Nevertheless, we
specify hereafter sufficient conditions for all the interchanges of derivatives and expectations to be valid in our main results.

To this end, we will repeatedly use the notation
\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)
defined in (
24
)
which, for every
\varepsilon
and
x
, is differentiable with respect to

(\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime})
on
\Theta\times\Phi^{2}
following the
differentiability conditions already assumed on

\theta\mapsto p_{\theta}(x)
,
(z,\theta)\mapsto p_{\theta}(z|x)
,

(z,\phi)\mapsto q_{\phi}(z|x)
and

\phi\mapsto f(\varepsilon,\phi;x)
in the beginning of

Section

3
. Furthermore,
\psi
will denote a component of the
\mathbb{R}^{a+b}
-valued variable
(\theta,\phi)=(\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{a},\phi_{1},\dots,\phi_{b})
and we will say that
\mathcal{V}
is a
\psi
-neighborhood of

(\theta,\phi)\in\mathbb{R}^{a+b}
if there exists
r>0
such that for
all
\psi^{\prime}\in(\psi-r,\psi+r)
, the vector obtained by replacing
\psi

by
\psi^{\prime}
in
(\theta,\phi)
belongs to
\mathcal{V}
.

The remaining of
Appendix

A
is then
concerned with providing sufficient conditions to ensure that the interchanges of derivatives and expectations necessary to establish the results of
Section

3.3
and
Section

3.4
are valid.

A.1
Interchanging Derivative and Expectation Signs in
Section

3.3

The first
assumption below is concerned with the REP gradient estimator.

(A
{}_{\mathrm{df}}^{\mathrm{REP}}
)

All
(\theta,\phi)\in\Theta\times\Phi
admit a

\psi
-neighborhood
\mathcal{V}\subset\Theta\times\Phi
such that,
for
\ell=0,1,2
,

\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\sup_{(\theta^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}}\left|\tilde{w}_{\theta^{\prime},\phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)^{\ell(1-\alpha)}\;\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\left(\log\tilde{w}_{\theta^{\prime},\phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)\right)\right|\right)<\infty\;.

(55)

We have the following result which will cover all the interchanges of
derivatives and expectations that are necessary for our study of the
REP gradient estimator in
Section

3.3
.

Proposition 2

Under
(A

df

REP

)
,
we have for all
(\theta,\phi)\in\Theta\times\Phi
,

\displaystyle\partial_{\psi}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\right)=\mathbb{E}\left(\partial_{\psi}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\right)\right)\;,

(56)

\displaystyle\partial_{\psi}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{2(1-\alpha)}\right)=\mathbb{E}\left(\partial_{\psi}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{2(1-\alpha)}\right)\right)\;.

(57)

Moreover, for all
N\geq 1
,

\partial_{\psi}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon_{j}\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\tilde{q}}\left(\log\left(\sum_{j=1}^{N}\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon_{j};x)^{1-\alpha}\right)\right)=\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon_{j}\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\tilde{q}}\left(\partial_{\psi}\log\left(\sum_{j=1}^{N}\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon_{j};x)^{1-\alpha}\right)\right)\;.

(58)

Proof.

Suppose that
(A

df

REP

)
holds. Observe that, for

\ell=1,2
, by (
24
), we have

\partial_{\psi}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{\ell(1-\alpha)}\right)=\ell(1-\alpha)\;w_{\theta,\phi}(f(\varepsilon,\phi;x);x)^{\ell(1-\alpha)}\;\partial_{\psi}\left(\log w_{\theta,\phi}(f(\varepsilon,\phi;x);x)\right)\;.

Thus, by the usual dominated
convergence argument for interchanging the expectation and derivative
signs, the condition in (
55
) for
\ell=1,2

leads to (
56
)
and (
57
), respectively.

Let now
N\geq 1
. To get (
58
), by the same
dominated convergence argument, it is
sufficient to show that all
(\theta,\phi)\in\Theta\times\Phi
admit a

\psi
-neighborhood
\mathcal{V}\subset\Theta\times\Phi
such that

\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon_{j}\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\tilde{q}}\left(\sup_{(\theta^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}}\left|\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\log\left(\sum_{j=1}^{N}\tilde{w}_{\theta^{\prime},\phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon_{j};x)^{1-\alpha}\right)\right|\right)<\infty\;.

Differentiating and using that the sup of a sum is bounded by the sum
of the sup from above, the latter condition is implied by having that for all
j=1,\dots,N
,

\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon_{j}\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\tilde{q}}\left(\sup_{(\theta^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}}\frac{\left|\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta^{\prime},\phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon_{j};x)^{1-\alpha}\right)\right|}{\sum_{k=1}^{N}\tilde{w}_{\theta^{\prime},\phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon_{k};x)^{1-\alpha}}\right)<\infty\;.

Using that
\tilde{w}_{\theta^{\prime},\phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon_{k};x)\geq 0

for all
k\neq j
, this ratio is bounded
from above by

\frac{\left|\partial_{\psi}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta^{\prime},\phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon_{j};x)^{1-\alpha}\right)\right|}{\tilde{w}_{\theta^{\prime},\phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon_{j};x)^{1-\alpha}}=(1-\alpha)\;\left|\partial_{\psi}\left(\log\tilde{w}_{\theta^{\prime},\phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon_{j};x)\right)\right|\;.

Hence assuming (
55
) with
\ell=0
ensures that the
previous condition holds and we have concluded the proof of (
58
).
∎

The next assumption is concerned
with the DREP gradient estimator and is only required for a component
\psi
of
the
\mathbb{R}^{b}
-valued variable
\phi=(\phi_{1},\dots,\phi_{b})
,

(A
{}_{\mathrm{df}}^{\mathrm{DREP}}
)

All
(\theta,\phi)
in
\Theta\times\Phi
admit a

\psi
-neighborhood
\mathcal{V}\subset\Theta\times\Phi
such that:

(i)

If
\alpha\in(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\sup_{(\theta,\phi^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}}\left|\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\right)\right|\right)<\infty\;.

(59)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\sup_{(\theta,\phi^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}}\left|\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\right)\right|\right)<\infty\;.

(60)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim q_{\phi}(\cdot|x)}\left(w_{\theta,\phi}(Z;x)^{1-\alpha}\,\sup_{(\theta,\phi^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}}\left|\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\left(\log q_{\phi^{\prime}}(Z|x)\right)\;\frac{q_{\phi^{\prime}}(Z|x)}{q_{\phi}(Z|x)}\right|\right)<\infty\;.

(61)

(ii)

If
\alpha=0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\sup_{(\theta,\phi^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}}\left|\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)^{2}\right)\right|\right)<\infty\;,

(62)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\sup_{(\theta,\phi^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}}\left|\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)^{2}\right)\right|\right)<\infty\;,

(63)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim q_{\phi}(\cdot|x)}\left(w_{\theta,\phi}(Z;x)^{2}\sup_{(\theta,\phi^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}}\left|\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\left(\log q_{\phi^{\prime}}(Z|x)\right)\;\frac{q_{\phi^{\prime}}(Z|x)}{q_{\phi}(Z|x)}\right|\right)<\infty\;.

(64)

We have the following result which will cover all the interchanges of
derivatives and expectations that are necessary for our study of the
DREP gradient estimator in
Section

3.3
.

Proposition 3

Let
\psi
denote a component of the

\mathbb{R}^{b}
-valued variable

\phi=(\phi_{1},\dots,\phi_{b})
. Under
(A

df

DREP

)
, we have the following
assertions for all
(\theta,\phi)
in
\Theta\times\Phi
.

(i)

If
\alpha\in(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle\left.\left[\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\right)\right]\right|_{\phi^{\prime}=\phi}=\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\left.\left[\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\right)\right]\right|_{\phi^{\prime}=\phi}\right),

(65)

\displaystyle\partial_{\psi}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\right)=\alpha\,\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\left.\left[\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\right)\right]\right|_{\phi^{\prime}=\phi}\right).

(66)

(ii)

If
\alpha=0
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{\psi}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{2}\right)=-\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\left.\left[\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)^{2}\right)\right]\right|_{\phi^{\prime}=\phi}\right).

(67)

Proof.

First observe that (
67
) in
Assertion
(ii)
corresponds
to (
66
) in
Assertion
(i)
with
\alpha

replaced by
-1
. In addition, the case
\alpha=0
in
Assumption
(A

df

DREP

)
corresponds to the case

\alpha\in(0,1)
with
\alpha
also replaced by
-1
.
We thus only have to prove that for any

\alpha\in(0,1)\cup\left\{-1\right\}
,
Conditions (
59
)–(
61
)
imply the identities (
65
)
and (
66
).

We will in fact prove that more generally: for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
, Conditions (
59
)–(
61
)
imply the identities (
65
)
and (
66
). The case
\alpha=1
is immediate so let us pick
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\left\{1\right\}
in the following and assume that (
59
)–(
61
) hold.
Note first that (
65
) is a simple interchange of the derivative and expectation signs which holds by dominated convergence
using (
60
). To then prove (
66
),
observe that by (
59
), we can interchange the derivative and expectation
signs on the left-hand side
of (
66
), that is: for all
\alpha\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\partial_{\psi}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\right)=\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\partial_{\psi}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\right)\right).

Since
\partial_{\psi}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\right)=\left.\left[\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\right)+\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi^{\prime},\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\right)\right]\right|_{\phi^{\prime}=\phi}
, to obtain (
66
) it remains to prove that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha-1}\,\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\left.\left[\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi^{\prime},\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\right)\right]\right|_{\phi^{\prime}=\phi}\right)=\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\left.\left[\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\right)\right]\right|_{\phi^{\prime}=\phi}\right)\;.

(68)

Observe then that, using (
24
), it holds that

\frac{1}{\alpha-1}\left.\left[\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi^{\prime},\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\right)\right]\right|_{\phi^{\prime}=\phi}=\,\left.\left[w_{\theta,\phi}(z;x)^{1-\alpha}\frac{\partial_{\psi}q_{\phi}(z)}{q_{\phi}(z|x)}\right]\right|_{z=f(\varepsilon,\phi;x)}\;.

Hence the left-hand side of (
68
) reads

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\left.\left[w_{\theta,\phi}(z;x)^{1-\alpha}\frac{\partial_{\psi}q_{\phi}(z|x)}{q_{\phi}(z|x)}\right]\right|_{z=f(\varepsilon,\phi;x)}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim q_{\phi}(\cdot|x)}\left(w_{\theta,\phi}(Z;x)^{1-\alpha}\frac{\partial_{\psi}q_{\phi}(Z|x)}{q_{\phi}(Z|x)}\right)

\displaystyle=\int w_{\theta,\phi}(Z;x)^{1-\alpha}\,\partial_{\psi}q_{\phi}(Z|x)\;\nu(\mathrm{d}z)

and the proof of (
68
) will be concluded if we can prove that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\left.\left[\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\right)\right]\right|_{\phi^{\prime}=\phi}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(a)}}}{{=}}\left.\left[\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}{\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\right)}\right]\right|_{\phi^{\prime}=\phi}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(b)}}}{{=}}\left.\left[\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim q_{\phi^{\prime}}(\cdot|x)}\left(w_{\theta,\phi}(Z;x)^{1-\alpha}\right)\right]\right|_{\phi^{\prime}=\phi}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(c)}}}{{=}}\int w_{\theta,\phi}(Z;x)^{1-\alpha}\,\partial_{\psi}q_{\phi}(Z|x)\;\nu(\mathrm{d}z).

The equality (a) follows from interchanging the integral and the
derivative signs again thanks to
(
60
) and the equality (b)
follows from the reparameterization trick. As for the equality (c),
it is obtained by interchanging the integral and the derivative
thanks to the fact that for a
\psi
-neighborhood
\mathcal{V}
of

(\theta,\phi)
,

\displaystyle\int w_{\theta,\phi}(z;x)^{1-\alpha}\,\sup_{(\theta,\phi^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}}\left|\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}q_{\phi^{\prime}}(z|x)\right|\;\nu(\mathrm{d}z)<\infty\;,

which holds using (
61
) and that

\sup_{(\theta,\phi^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}}\left|\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}q_{\phi^{\prime}}(Z|x)\right|=\sup_{(\theta,\phi^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}}\left|\left(\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\log q_{\phi^{\prime}}(Z|x)\right)\,\frac{{q_{\phi^{\prime}}(Z|x)}}{q_{\phi}(Z|x)}\right|\,q_{\phi}(Z|x)\;.

The proof of
Proposition

3
is thus
concluded.
∎

The conclusions from
Proposition

3
will be
used to express the constants in the asymptotic behavior of the SNR of
the DREP estimator, see the proof of
Theorem

4
.

A.2
Interchanging Derivative and Expectation Signs in
Section

3.4

We now examine the specific case where the Gaussian
assumption
(B3)
holds. In this special
case, we rely on the following assumption for the REP
gradient estimator.

(B
{}_{\mathrm{df}}^{\mathrm{REP}}
)

All
(\theta,\phi)
in

\Theta\times\Phi
admit a
\psi
-neighborhood
\mathcal{V}\subset\Theta\times\Phi
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\sup_{(\theta^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}}\left(\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\log\tilde{w}_{\theta^{\prime},\phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)\right)^{2}\right)<\infty\;,

(69)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\sup_{(\theta^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}}\left(\log\tilde{w}_{\theta^{\prime},\phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)\right)^{2}\right)<\infty\;.

(70)

We have the following result which will cover all the interchanges of derivatives and expectations that are necessary for our study of the REP gradient estimator in
Proposition

1
.

Proposition 4

Let
\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}
. Under
(B3)

and
(B

df

REP

)
, we have that:
for all

(\theta,\phi)\in\Theta\times\Phi
,

\displaystyle\partial_{\psi}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\log\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)\right)=\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\partial_{\psi}\log\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)\right)

(71)

\displaystyle\partial_{\psi}\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\left(\log\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)\right)^{2}\right)=\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\partial_{\psi}\left(\left(\log\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)\right)^{2}\right)\right)

(72)

and
\left(\left(\log\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x),\partial_{\psi}\log\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)\right)\right)_{(\theta,\phi)\in\Theta\times\Phi}
is a Gaussian process.

Proof.

Using the dominated convergence theorem, (
69
) in
(B

df

REP

)

implies (
71
) but also that

\partial_{\psi}\log\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)
can be seen as the

L^{2}
limit of a linear combination of the process

\left(\log\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)\right)_{(\theta,\phi)\in\Theta\times\Phi}
.

It
follows by (
36
) that

\left(\left(\log\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x),\partial_{\psi}\log\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)\right)\right)_{(\theta,\phi)\in\Theta\times\Phi}

is a Gaussian process. As for (
72
),
it follows from the dominated convergence theorem and

\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\sup_{(\theta^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}}{\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\left(\left(\log\tilde{w}_{\theta^{\prime},\phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)\right)^{2}\right)}\right)\\
\leq 2\left(\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\sup_{(\theta^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}}\left(\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\log\tilde{w}_{\theta^{\prime},\phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)\right)^{2}\right)\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\sup_{(\theta^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}}\left(\log\tilde{w}_{\theta^{\prime},\phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)\right)^{2}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}<\infty\;,

where the first inequality uses the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the
second is a consequence of (
69
)
and (
70
).
∎

Appendix B
Deferred Proofs of
Section

3.2

In this section, we let
(X_{1},W_{1}),\dots,(X_{N},W_{N})
be i.i.d. with the same distribution as a generic pair
(X,W)
valued in
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
and we set: for
all
v\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\phi(v)=\mathbb{E}\left(\mathrm{e}^{-v\,W}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\phi_{1}(v)=\mathbb{E}\left(X\,\mathrm{e}^{-v\,W}\right),

(73)

\displaystyle\overline{W}_{N}:=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}W_{i}\quad\text{and}\quad\overline{X}_{N}:=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}X_{i},

(74)

\displaystyle\overline{X^{2}}_{N}:=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}X_{i}^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\overline{X}^{(i\neq j)}_{N}:=\frac{2}{N(N-1)}\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq N}X_{i}X_{j},

(75)

\displaystyle\overline{X^{c}}_{N}:=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}X_{i}^{c}\quad\text{and}\quad\overline{X^{c}}^{(i\neq j)}_{N}:=\frac{2}{N(N-1)}\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq N}X_{i}^{c}X_{j}^{c},

(76)

\displaystyle\text{where we set $X_{i}^{c}:=X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X)$}.

B.1
Preliminary Lemmas

In the following, we provide two preliminary lemmas that gather useful properties of the functions
\phi
and
\phi_{1}
defined in (
73
).

Lemma 2

(Properties of the Laplace transform
\phi
)

The following assertions
hold.

(i)

The function
\phi
is continuous and bounded on

\mathbb{R}_{+}
.

(ii)

Assume that
\mathbb{E}\left(W\right)=1
. Then, for all

N\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and all

u\geq 0
,

\left(\phi\left(\frac{u}{N}\right)\right)^{N}-\mathrm{e}^{-u}\geq 0\;.

(iii)

Assume that
\mathbb{E}\left(W\right)=1
and
\mathbb{E}\left(W^{2}\right)<\infty
. Then, for all

N\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and all

u\in\left[0,\frac{N}{\mathbb{E}\left(W^{2}\right)}\right]
,

\displaystyle 0\leq N\left(\left(\phi\left(\frac{u}{N}\right)\right)^{N}-\mathrm{e}^{-u}\right)\leq\mathrm{e}^{-u/2}\;.

(77)

Furthermore,
for all

u\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}N\left(\left(\phi\left(\frac{u}{N}\right)\right)^{N}-\mathrm{e}^{-u}\right)=\frac{\mathbb{V}\left(W\right)}{2}\,u^{2}\;\mathrm{e}^{-u}\;.

(78)

(iv)

Assume that there exists
\eta>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{t>0}\left(t^{\eta}\;\mathbb{E}\left(\mathrm{e}^{-t\,W}\right)\right)<\infty\;.

(79)

Then, for any exponent
m>0
, there exists
c,p>0
such that: for all
q\geq p
and
v\geq 0
,

\left(\phi\left(\frac{v}{q}\right)\right)^{q}\leq 1\wedge\left(c\,v^{-m}\right)\;.

Proof.

The assertion
(i)
is immediate since
\phi
is valued in
[0,1]
and is continuous by the dominated convergence theorem. In addition,
(ii)
follows from Jensen’s inequality applied to the convex function
W\mapsto\mathrm{e}^{-{uW}/{N}}
and paired up with the fact that
\mathbb{E}(W)=1
by assumption. We now move on to the proof of
(iii)
and
(iv)
.

•

Proof of
(iii)
.

First note that for all
x\geq 0
,

\displaystyle-\frac{x^{2}}{2}\left(1-\mathrm{e}^{-x}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{e}^{-x}-1+x-\frac{x^{2}}{2}\leq 0

(80)

since for all
x\geq 0
:
1-\mathrm{e}^{-t}\leq 1-\mathrm{e}^{-x}
for
0\leq t\leq x
and

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{-x}-1+x-\frac{x^{2}}{2}=-\int_{0}^{x}(x-t)(1-\mathrm{e}^{-t})\mathrm{d}t.

Applying the second inequality in (
80
) with
x=vW
and taking expectation yields:

\phi(v)\leq 1-v+\frac{v^{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}(W^{2}),\quad v\geq 0.

We deduce from the inequality above and
(ii)
that: for all
u\geq 0

and
N\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,

0\leq\left(\phi\left(\frac{u}{N}\right)\right)^{N}-\mathrm{e}^{-u}\leq\mathrm{e}^{-u}\left(\mathrm{e}^{u+N\ln\left(1-\frac{u}{N}+\frac{u^{2}}{2N^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left(W^{2}\right)\right)}-1\right)\;.

Since
\ln(1+x)\leq x
, we then get that for all
u\geq 0
and
N\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,

0\leq\left(\phi\left(\frac{u}{N}\right)\right)^{N}-\mathrm{e}^{-u}\leq\mathrm{e}^{-u}\left(\mathrm{e}^{\frac{u^{2}}{2N}\mathbb{E}\left(W^{2}\right)}-1\right)\leq\mathrm{e}^{-u\left(1-\frac{u}{2N}\mathbb{E}\left(W^{2}\right)\right)}\;.

Restricting
u
to the set
\left[0,\frac{N}{\mathbb{E}\left(W^{2}\right)}\right]
yields (
77
).

Now let
u\geq 0
be given. Applying (
80
) with

x=u\,W/N
and taking expectation, we get that for all
N\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,

\left|\phi\left(\frac{u}{N}\right)-1+\frac{u}{N}-\frac{\mathbb{E}\left(W^{2}\right)}{2}\,\frac{u^{2}}{N^{2}}\right|\leq\frac{u^{2}}{2N^{2}}\;\mathbb{E}\left(W^{2}\left(1-\mathrm{e}^{-W\,u/N}\right)\right)\;.

By dominated convergence, the expectation in the upper bound
converges to 0 as
N\to\infty
. Hence we have, as
N\to\infty
,

\ln\phi\left(\frac{u}{N}\right)=\ln\left(1-\frac{u}{N}+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left(W^{2}\right)}{2}\,\frac{u^{2}}{N^{2}}+o\left(\frac{1}{N^{2}}\right)\right)=-\frac{u}{N}+\frac{\mathbb{V}\left(W\right)}{2}\,\frac{u^{2}}{N^{2}}+o\left(\frac{1}{N^{2}}\right)\;,

where used that
\ln(1+x)=x-x^{2}/2+o(x^{2})
as
x\to 0
and
\mathbb{E}\left(W^{2}\right)-1=\mathbb{V}\left(W\right)
.
It follows that, as
N\to\infty
,

N\left(\left(\phi\left(\frac{u}{N}\right)\right)^{N}-\mathrm{e}^{-u}\right)=N\,\mathrm{e}^{-u}\,\left(\mathrm{e}^{N\ln\phi\left(\frac{u}{N}\right)+u}-1\right)=\mathrm{e}^{-u}\,N\,\left(\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\mathbb{V}\left(W\right)}{2}\,\frac{u^{2}}{N}+o\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)}-1\right)\;,

which yields the given limit (
78
) using that
\mathrm{e}^{x}=1+x+o(x)
as
x\to 0
.

•

Proof of
(iv)
.
First note that for any
v\geq 0
,
p\mapsto\left(\phi\left(\frac{v}{p}\right)\right)^{p}
is non-increasing as
p
increases (since for all
q\geq p
,
\left(\phi\left(\frac{v}{p}\right)\right)^{p}=\left(\phi\left(\frac{v}{q}\frac{q}{p}\right)\right)^{p}\geq\left(\phi\left(\frac{v}{q}\right)\right)^{q}
by Jensen’s inequality). Furthermore, by (
79
), we have: for any
p>0
,

c_{1}(p):=\sup_{t>0}\left((t/p)^{\eta}\;\phi(t/p)\right)<\infty\;.

Let
m>0
and take
p=m/\eta
. Then we have for all
q\geq p
and
v\geq 0
,

\left(\phi\left(\frac{v}{q}\right)\right)^{q}\leq\left(\phi\left(\frac{v}{p}\right)\right)^{p}\leq(v/p)^{-p\,\eta}\;\left(\sup_{t>0}(t/p)^{\eta}\;\phi\left(\frac{t}{p}\right)\right)^{p}=c\;v^{-m}\;,

where we set
c=p^{p\eta}\;\left(c_{1}(p)\right)^{p}
, hence the proof of
(iv)
is completed.

∎

Lemma 3

(Properties of
\phi_{1}
)

The following assertions
hold.

(i)

Assume that

\mathbb{E}(|X|)<\infty
. Then,
\phi_{1}
is continuous and bounded on

\mathbb{R}_{+}
.

(ii)

Assume that
\mathbb{E}(X)=0

and
\mathbb{E}(|X|\,W)<\infty
. Then, for all
u\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{N\geq 1}\left(N\left|\phi_{1}\left(\frac{u}{N}\right)+\mathbb{E}\left(XW\right)\;\frac{u}{N}\right|\right)\leq\mathbb{E}(|X|W)u\;,

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}N\left|\phi_{1}\left(\frac{u}{N}\right)+\mathbb{E}\left(XW\right)\frac{u}{N}\right|=0\;.

(iii)

Assume that

\mathbb{E}(X)=0
and
\mathbb{E}(|X|\,W^{\delta})<\infty
with
0<\delta<1
. Then, for all
u\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{N\geq 1}\left(N^{\delta}\left|\phi_{1}\left(\frac{u}{N}\right)\right|\right)\leq\mathbb{E}\left(|X|\,W^{\delta}\right)\;u^{\delta}\;,

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}N^{\delta}\left|\phi_{1}\left(\frac{u}{N}\right)\right|=0\;.

Proof.

The assertion
(i)
is immediate since
\phi_{1}
is valued in
[-\mathbb{E}(X_{-}),\mathbb{E}(X_{+})]
and it is
continuous by the dominated convergence theorem when
\mathbb{E}(|X|)<\infty
. As for
(ii)
, first note that for all
x\geq 0
,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{e}^{-x}-1+x\leq x\left(1-\mathrm{e}^{-x}\right),

(81)

since for all
x\geq 0
,
1-\mathrm{e}^{-t}\leq 1-\mathrm{e}^{-x}
for
0\leq t\leq x
and

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{-x}-1+x=\int_{0}^{x}\left(1-\mathrm{e}^{-t}\right)\mathrm{d}t.

Using
\mathbb{E}(X)=0
and
\mathbb{E}(|X|\,W)<\infty
, we have, for all
u\geq 0
and
N\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\left|\phi_{1}\left(\frac{u}{N}\right)+\frac{u\,\mathbb{E}\left(X\,W\right)}{N}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbb{E}\left(X\left(\mathrm{e}^{-u\,W/N}-1+u\,W/N\right)\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left(\left|X\right|\left(\mathrm{e}^{-u\,W/N}-1+u\,W/N\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{u}{N}\;\mathbb{E}\left(\,|X|\,W\;\left(1-\mathrm{e}^{-u\,W/N}\right)\right)\;,

where in the last inequality we applied (
81
) with

x=u\,W/N
. The two claims of the assertion
(ii)
easily follow.

Moving on to
(iii)
, we use
that, for all
x\geq 0
,
0\leq 1-\mathrm{e}^{-x}\leq x
, which implies

0\leq 1-\mathrm{e}^{-x}=\left(1-\mathrm{e}^{-x}\right)^{\delta}\;\left(1-\mathrm{e}^{-x}\right)^{1-\delta}\leq x^{\delta}\;\left(1-\mathrm{e}^{-x}\right)^{1-\delta}\;.

We get that, since
\mathbb{E}(X)=0
, for all
u\geq 0
and
N\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\left|\phi_{1}\left(\frac{u}{N}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbb{E}\left(X\left(\mathrm{e}^{-uW/N}-1\right)\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left(|X|\left(1-\mathrm{e}^{-uW/N}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{u}{N}\right)^{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left(|X|\,{W}^{\delta}\,\left(1-\mathrm{e}^{-uW/N}\right)^{1-\delta}\right)

and the desired asssertions in

(iii)
follow under
\mathbb{E}\left(|X|\,{W}^{\delta}\right)<\infty
using
that
\left(1-\mathrm{e}^{-uW/N}\right)^{1-\delta}
is bounded by 1 and vanishes as

N\to\infty
for
u\,W>0
.
∎

Next, we provide useful equivalences for the condition (
79
).

B.2
Useful Equivalences

Proposition 5

Set
\overline{W}_{N}=N^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}W_{i}
for all

N\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}
, where
W_{1},\ldots,W_{N}
are positive
i.i.d. random variables distributed as a generic random variable
W
. Then the following assertions hold.

(i)

For all
\mu>0
, we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left(\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{-\mu}\right)<\infty\Longleftrightarrow\exists N\geq 1\;,\,\mathbb{E}\left(\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{-\mu}\right)<\infty.

(82)

Furthermore, if the assertions of the equivalence (
82
) hold for some
\mu>0
and the distribution of
W
does not reduce to a Dirac measure, there exists
N_{0}\geq 1
such that
\mathbb{E}\left(\left(1/\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}\right)=\infty
for
1\leq N<N_{0}
and the sequence
\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\left(1/\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}\right)\right)_{N\geq N_{0}}
is strictly decreasing in
(0,\infty)
.

(ii)

For all
\eta>0
,
the two conditions (
17
)
and (
79
) are equivalent.

(iii)

If
condition (
79
) holds for
\eta>0
,
then
\mathbb{E}\left((\overline{W}_{N})^{-\mu}\right)<\infty
for all
\mu>0

and
N\geq 1
such that
N>\mu/\eta
.

(iv)

If
condition (
79
) holds for
\eta>0
, then

\mathbb{E}(W^{-\mu})<\infty
for
0<\mu<\eta
.

(v)

If

\mathbb{E}(W^{-\mu})<\infty
for
\mu>0
, then
condition (
17
) holds for
0<\eta\leq\mu
.

(vi)

If

\mathbb{E}\left(\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{-\mu}\right)<\infty
for some
\mu>0
and

N\geq 1
, then (
79
) hold with
\eta=\mu/N
.

(vii)

Suppose
that (
17
) holds for some
\eta>0
.
Then, for all
\mu>0
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left((1/\overline{W}_{N})^{\mu}\right)=\begin{cases}0&\text{ if $\mathbb{E}(W)=\infty$,}\\
\left(\mathbb{E}(W)\right)^{-\mu}&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

(83)

Proof.

We prove Assertions
(i)
–
(vii)
successively.

(i)

Proof of Assertion
(i)
.

Let
\Gamma
denote the Euler Gamma function, so that
\Gamma(\mu)=\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{\mu-1}\mathrm{e}^{-t}\;\mathrm{d}t
for all
\mu>0
. Following an idea of
Cressie et al., 1981
, we have: for all
N\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{-\mu}\right)

\displaystyle=(\Gamma(\mu))^{-1}\,\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{\mu-1}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathrm{e}^{-\overline{W}_{N}\,t}\right)\;\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=(\Gamma(\mu))^{-1}\,\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{\mu-1}\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\mathrm{e}^{-W\,t/N}\right)\right)^{N}\;\mathrm{d}t,

(84)

where we have used that, for all
x>0
,

x^{-\mu}=(\Gamma(\mu))^{-1}\,\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{\mu-1}\mathrm{e}^{-x\,t}\;\mathrm{d}t
and Tonelli’s theorem. For all
t\geq 0
and
N\geq 1
, by Jensen’s inequality and strict convexity of

x\mapsto x^{(N+1)/N}
on
(0,\infty)
, we have, for all
t>0
,

\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\mathrm{e}^{-W\,t/N}\right)\right)^{N}=\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\left(\mathrm{e}^{-W\,t/(N+1)}\right)^{(N+1)/N}\right)\right)^{N}\geq\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\mathrm{e}^{-W\,t/(N+1)}\right)\right)^{N+1}\;,

with equality if and only if both sides of the inequality are
infinite or
\mathrm{e}^{-W\,t/(N+1)}
is equal to its
mean a.s. and we can thus deduce Assertion
(i)
.

(ii)

Proof of
Assertion
(ii)
.
Let
\eta>0
. We first assume that

\sup_{t>0}\left(t^{\eta}\;\mathbb{E}\left(\mathrm{e}^{-t\,W}\right)\right)<\infty

(85)

holds and
show

\sup_{u>0}\left(u^{-\eta}\;\mathbb{P}\left(W\leq u\right)\right)<\infty.

(86)

We have, for all

u>0
, by Markov’s inequality,

\mathbb{P}\left(W\leq u\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(\mathrm{e}^{\eta(1-W/u)}\geq 1\right)\leq\mathrm{e}^{\eta}\;\mathbb{E}\left(\mathrm{e}^{-\eta\,W/u}\right)\leq\mathrm{e}^{\eta}\;C\,\eta^{-\eta}\,u^{\eta},

where
C
is the sup in the left-hand side
of (
85
). Hence (
86
) follows. Let us now
assume (
86
) and show
that (
85
) holds.
We have, for all
t>0
,

\mathbb{E}\left(\mathrm{e}^{-t\,W}\right)=t\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{e}^{-t\,u}\;\mathbb{P}\left(W\leq u\right)\;\mathrm{d}u=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{e}^{-v}\;\mathbb{P}\left(W\leq v/t\right)\;\mathrm{d}v\;.

Let
C
now denote the (finite) sup in the left-hand side
of (
86
), so that

\mathbb{P}\left(W\leq u\right)\leq C\,u^{\eta}
for all
u>0
. Using this bound
in the previous integral, we get that, for all
t>0
,

\mathbb{E}\left(\mathrm{e}^{-t\,W}\right)\leq C\,t^{-\eta}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{e}^{-v}\;v^{\eta}\;\mathrm{d}v\;.

Since the latter integral is a finite constant, this concludes the
proof of
(ii)
.

(iii)

Proof of
Assertion
(iii)
.
Suppose
that (
79
) holds for some

\eta>0
. Then there exists
c>0
such that, for all
t\geq 0
,

\mathbb{E}\left(\mathrm{e}^{-W\,t}\right)\leq 1\wedge\left(c\,t^{-\eta}\right)
.
Hence for
\mu>0
and
N\geq 1
such that
N>\mu/\eta
, the right-hand side
of (
84
) is finite and

\mathbb{E}\left((1/\overline{W}_{N})^{\mu}\right)
as well.

(iv)

Proof of Assertion
(iv)
.

This is a special case of
(iii)

imposing
N=1
.

(v)

Proof of
Assertion
(v)
.
Suppose that

\mathbb{E}(W^{-\mu})<\infty
for some
\mu>0
. Then
\mathbb{P}(W=0)=0
and, for all
u>0
,

\mathbb{P}(W\leq u)=\mathbb{P}(W^{-\mu}u^{\mu}\geq 1)\leq u^{\mu}\mathbb{E}(W^{-\mu})\;,

where we used the Markov inequality.

(vi)

Proof of
Assertion
(vi)
.

Applying Assertions
(v)
and
(ii)

successively with
W

replaced by
\overline{W}_{N}
, the
given assumption implies

\sup_{t>0}\left(t^{\mu}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathrm{e}^{-t\overline{W}_{N}}\right)\right)<\infty\;.

Since

\mathbb{E}\left(\mathrm{e}^{-t\overline{W}_{N}}\right)=\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\mathrm{e}^{-t\,W/N}\right)\right)^{N}
, we
get, setting
u=t/N
,

\sup_{u>0}\left(u^{\mu}\,\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\mathrm{e}^{-u\,W}\right)\right)^{N}\right)<\infty\;.

The claimed implication follows.

(vii)

Proof of
Assertion
(vii)
.
By the
strong law of large numbers we have
\overline{W}_{N}\to\mathbb{E}\left(W\right)

as
N\to\infty
a.s., including in the case where

\mathbb{E}\left(W\right)=\infty
. Hence the sequence

\left((1/\overline{W}_{N})^{\mu}\right)_{N\geq 1}
converges to the right-hand
side of (
83
) a.s. To conclude the proof of
Assertion
(vii)
, it suffices to
show that, for all
\mu>0
, the sequence

\left((1/\overline{W}_{N})^{\mu}\right)_{N\geq N_{0}}
is uniformly integrable
for some
N_{0}\geq 1

(see for example Theorem 3.5 of
Billingsley, 1971
)
. By the Markov inequality, the uniform integrability then follows from the fact
that: for some
\epsilon>0
,

\limsup_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left(\left((1/\overline{W}_{N})^{\mu}\right)^{1+\epsilon}\right)<\infty.

In other words, the uniform integrability will follow from

\limsup_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left((1/\overline{W}_{N})^{\mu^{\prime}}\right)<\infty,

with
\mu^{\prime}=\mu(1+\epsilon)>\mu
. By Assertion
(iii)
, the assertions of the equivalence (
82
) hold for
all
\mu>0
. The proof is concluded by taking
\mu^{\prime}=\mu(1+\epsilon)
in lieu of
\mu
in the assertions of the equivalence (
82
).

∎

Remark 1

Assertion
(ii)
in

Proposition

5
is related to the famous
Abelian and Tauberian theorems for the Laplace transform of a
probability measure on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
, see
Feller, 1991
, Section XIII.5
.

Remark 2

Assertion
(i)
in
Proposition

5
can be deduced from the proof of
Daudel et al., 2023
, Lemma 4
. Here we provided a simpler
proof of this assertion and, more importantly, we obtained the novel assertions
(ii)
–
(vii)
.

B.3
Asymptotic Behavior of Sample Means Ratios: Key Special Cases

In this section, we investigate the asymptotic behavior of sample means ratios in some special cases that are pivotal to prove

Theorem

1
. We start with a preliminary lemma.

Lemma 4

Suppose that (
79
) holds for some
\eta>0
and let
\mu>0
. Then the following assertions hold.

(i)

If
X
is valued in
\mathbb{R}_{+}
, then the two following identities
hold for
N\geq 1
and
N\geq 2
respectively:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X}_{N}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{t^{\mu-1}}{\Gamma(\mu)}\,\phi_{1}\left(\frac{t}{N}\right)\;\left(\phi\left(\frac{t}{N}\right)\right)^{N-1}\mathrm{d}t,

(87)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X}^{(i\neq j)}_{N}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{t^{\mu-1}}{\Gamma(\mu)}\left(\phi_{1}\left(\frac{t}{N}\right)\right)^{2}\left(\phi\left(\frac{t}{N}\right)\right)^{N-2}\mathrm{d}t.

(88)

(ii)

If
\mathbb{E}(|X|)<\infty
,
then there exists
N_{0}\geq 3
such that for all
N\geq N_{0}
, the
identities (
87
)
and (
88
) hold with well
defined and finite integrals. Moreover the following identity holds for all

N\geq N_{0}
:

\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X^{c}}^{(i\neq j)}_{N}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)=\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X}^{(i\neq j)}_{N}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)-2\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}(X)\,\overline{X}_{N}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)+\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\left(\mathbb{E}(X)\right)^{2}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)\;.

(89)

Proof.

We prove
(i)
and
(ii)
sequentially.

(i)

Proof of
(i)
.

Proceeding as in (
84
) and using that

x^{-\mu}=(\Gamma(\mu))^{-1}\,\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{\mu-1}\mathrm{e}^{-x\,t}\;\mathrm{d}t
for all
x>0
, we have by
Tonelli’s theorem (since
\overline{X}_{N}
and
\overline{W}_{N}
are
nonnegative): for all
N\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X}_{N}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)

\displaystyle=(\Gamma(\mu))^{-1}\,\mathbb{E}\left(\overline{X}_{N}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{\mu-1}\;\mathrm{e}^{-\overline{W}_{N}\,t}\mathrm{d}t\right)

\displaystyle=(\Gamma(\mu))^{-1}\,\mathbb{E}\left(X_{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{\mu-1}\;\mathrm{e}^{-\overline{W}_{N}\,t}\mathrm{d}t\right)

\displaystyle=(\Gamma(\mu))^{-1}\,\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{\mu-1}\left(\mathbb{E}\left(X_{1}\mathrm{e}^{-\overline{W}_{N}\,t}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=(\Gamma(\mu))^{-1}\,\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{\mu-1}\,\phi_{1}(t/N)\,\left(\phi(t/N)\right)^{N-1}\mathrm{d}t.

As for (
88
), it is obtained similarly by using that for all
t>0
and
N\geq 2
,

\frac{2}{N(N-1)}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq N}\mathbb{E}\left(X_{i}X_{j}\mathrm{e}^{-\overline{W}_{N}\,t}\right)=\left(\phi_{1}\left(\frac{t}{N}\right)\right)^{2}\left(\phi\left(\frac{t}{N}\right)\right)^{N-2}\;,

so that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X}^{(i\neq j)}_{N}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)=(\Gamma(\mu))^{-1}\,\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{\mu-1}\,\phi_{1}(t/N)^{2}\,\left(\phi(t/N)\right)^{N-2}\mathrm{d}t.

(ii)

Proof of
(ii)
.

Setting
u=(N-1)t/N
for
N\geq 2
and
u=(N-2)t/N
for
N\geq 3

respectively yield that (
87
)
and (
88
) can be equivalently
written as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X}_{N}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)=\left(\frac{N}{N-1}\right)^{\mu}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{u^{\mu-1}}{\Gamma(\mu)}\,\phi_{1}\left(\frac{u}{N-1}\right)\;\left(\phi\left(\frac{u}{N-1}\right)\right)^{N-1}\mathrm{d}u,

(90)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X}^{(i\neq j)}_{N}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)=\left(\frac{N}{N-2}\right)^{\mu}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{u^{\mu-1}}{\Gamma(\mu)}\left(\phi_{1}\left(\frac{u}{N-2}\right)\right)^{2}\left(\phi\left(\frac{u}{N-2}\right)\right)^{N-2}\mathrm{d}u.

(91)

Based on the proof
(i)
, (
90
) will be valid when
X
is not valued in
\mathbb{R}_{+}
provided that Fubini’s theorem applies. In other words, (
90
) will hold if
\mathbb{E}\left({\overline{\left|X\right|}_{N}}/{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)<\infty
where
\overline{\left|X\right|}_{N}=N^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left|X_{i}\right|
. Denoting
\tilde{\phi}_{1}(v)=\mathbb{E}\left(|X|\mathrm{e}^{-v\,W}\right)
, we have by applying (
90
) that

\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{|X|}_{N}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)=\left(\frac{N}{N-1}\right)^{\mu}\frac{1}{\Gamma(\mu)}\int_{0}^{\infty}u^{\mu-1}\,\tilde{\phi}_{1}\left(\frac{u}{N-1}\right)\;\left(\phi\left(\frac{u}{N-1}\right)\right)^{N-1}\mathrm{d}u\;.

Since
\mathbb{E}(|X|)<\infty
by assumption, the function
\tilde{\phi}_{1}

is bounded on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
. Furthermore,

(iv)
of
Lemma

2

with
m=\mu+1

yields that: there exist
c>0
and
N_{0}\geq 2

such that, for all
N\geq N_{0}
,

\displaystyle\left|u^{\mu-1}\,\tilde{\phi}_{1}\left(\frac{u}{N-1}\right)\left(\phi\left(\frac{u}{N-1}\right)\right)^{N-1}\right|\leq\mathbb{E}(|X|)\penalty\ u^{\mu-1}\left[1\wedge\left(c\,u^{-\mu-1}\right)\right],\quad u>0.

(92)

Consequently

\mathbb{E}\left({\overline{\left|X\right|}_{N}}/{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)<\infty
for
all
N\geq N_{0}
. Hence, for all
N\geq N_{0}
Fubini’s theorem applies
and (
90
) holds, and so does its
equivalent form (
87
). The proof is concluded by applying the same line of reasoning to (
91
) and using that there exist
c>0
and
N_{0}\geq 3
such that, for all
N\geq N_{0}
,

\displaystyle\left|u^{\mu-1}\,\tilde{\phi}_{1}\left(\frac{u}{N-2}\right)^{2}\left(\phi\left(\frac{u}{N-2}\right)\right)^{N-2}\right|\leq\left(\mathbb{E}(|X|)\right)^{2}\penalty\ u^{\mu-1}\left[1\wedge\left(c\,u^{-\mu-1}\right)\right],\quad u>0.

(93)

Hence we get (
88
). We
conclude by observing that the
identity (
89
) is obtained by
applying (
87
)
and (
88
) to
X
and
X_{i}

replaced by
X^{c}=X-\mathbb{E}(X)
and
X_{i}^{c}
by
X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X)
(with
corresponding similar upper bounds in (
92
) and (
93
)) and noting that

\phi_{1}^{c}(u)=\mathbb{E}\left(X^{c}\mathrm{e}^{-u\,W}\right)
satisfies

\phi_{1}^{c}=\phi_{1}-\mathbb{E}(X)\,\phi
.

∎

Proposition 6

Suppose that (
79
) holds for some
\eta>0
and let
\mu>0
. Then the following assertions hold.

(i)

If
\mathbb{E}(W)=1
and
\mathbb{E}(W^{2})<\infty
, then

\lim_{N\to\infty}N\,\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{-\mu}\right)-1\right)=\frac{(\mu+1)\mu}{2}\,\mathbb{V}\left(W\right)\;.

(ii)

If
\mathbb{E}(|X|)<\infty
, then

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X}_{N}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)=\frac{\mathbb{E}(X)}{\left(\mathbb{E}(W)\right)^{\mu}}\quad\text{and}\quad\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X}^{(i\neq j)}_{N}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)=\frac{\left(\mathbb{E}(X)\right)^{2}}{\left(\mathbb{E}(W)\right)^{\mu}}\;.

(iii)

If
\mathbb{E}(X)=0
and

\mathbb{E}(|X|\,W)<\infty
, then

\lim_{N\to\infty}N\,\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X}_{N}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)=-\frac{\mu\;\mathbb{C}\mathrm{ov}\left(X,W\right)}{\left(\mathbb{E}(W)\right)^{\mu+1}}\;.

(iv)

If
\mathbb{E}(X)=0
and
\mathbb{E}(|X|^{2})<\infty
, then

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\left(\overline{X}_{N}\right)^{2}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)=0\;.

(v)

If
\mathbb{E}(X)=0
and

\mathbb{E}\left(|X|\,W^{\delta}\right))<\infty
with
0<\delta<1
, then

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}N^{\delta}\,\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X}_{N}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)=0.

Further assuming that
\delta=1/2
and
\mathbb{E}(|X|^{2})<\infty
, it holds that

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}N\,\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\left(\overline{X}_{N}\right)^{2}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)=\frac{\mathbb{V}\left(X\right)}{\left(\mathbb{E}(W)\right)^{\mu}}\;.

(94)

Proof.

We prove Assertions

(i)
–
(v)

successively.

(i)

Proof of
(i)
.

Using (
84
) and the definition of the Euler Gamma function we obtain that, for all
N\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,

\displaystyle N\,\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{-\mu}\right)-1\right)

\displaystyle=(\Gamma(\mu))^{-1}\,\int_{0}^{\infty}u^{\mu-1}\,N\,\left(\left(\phi\left(\frac{u}{N}\right)\right)^{N}-\mathrm{e}^{-u}\right)\;\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle=:(\Gamma(\mu))^{-1}\,\left(A_{N}+B_{N}\right)\;,

where
A_{N}
and
B_{N}
are defined by separating the integral in two
parts and setting

\displaystyle A_{N}=\int_{0}^{\infty}u^{\mu-1}\,N\,\left(\left(\phi\left(\frac{u}{N}\right)\right)^{N}-\mathrm{e}^{-u}\right)\mathds{1}_{u\in\left[0,\frac{N}{\mathbb{E}\left(W^{2}\right)}\right]}\;\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle B_{N}=\int_{0}^{\infty}u^{\mu-1}\,N\,\left(\left(\phi\left(\frac{u}{N}\right)\right)^{N}-\mathrm{e}^{-u}\right)\mathds{1}_{u\in\left(\frac{N}{\mathbb{E}\left(W^{2}\right)},\infty\right)}\;\mathrm{d}u.

Using next (
77
) and (
78
) of
Lemma

2
, we obtain that the dominated convergence theorem applies and leads to

\lim_{N\to\infty}A_{N}=\frac{\mathbb{V}\left(W\right)}{2}\,\int_{0}^{\infty}u^{\mu+1}\;\mathrm{e}^{-u}\;\mathrm{d}u=\frac{\mathbb{V}\left(W\right)\,\Gamma(\mu+2)}{2}.

As for
B_{N}
,
(iv)
of
Lemma

2
with
m=\mu+2
yields that: there exist

c>0
and
N_{0}\geq 1
such that, for all
N\geq N_{0}
,

\left(\phi\left(\frac{u}{N}\right)\right)^{N}-\mathrm{e}^{-u}\leq\left(\phi\left(\frac{u}{N}\right)\right)^{N}\leq c\;u^{-\mu-2}\;.

Combining this with
(ii)
of

Lemma

2
, gives us that, for all
N\geq N_{0}
,

0\leq B_{N}\leq c\,N\,\int_{\frac{N}{\mathbb{E}\left(W^{2}\right)}}^{\infty}u^{-3}\;\mathrm{d}u\leq\frac{c}{2}\;\left(\mathbb{E}\left(W^{2}\right)\right)^{2}\;N^{-1}\;.

Thus
B_{N}
converges to zero and the given limit is proved using the
well known identity
\Gamma(\mu+2)=(\mu+1)\,\mu\,\Gamma(\mu)
.

(ii)

Proof of
(ii)
.
Let
us first consider the case where
\mathbb{E}(X)=0
, that is, we show that

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X}_{N}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)=\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X}^{(i\neq j)}_{N}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)=0\;.

(95)

By
(ii)
of

Lemma

4
, we can use the
identities (
87
)
and (
88
) for
N
large
enough, or their equivalent versions (
90
)
and (
91
).
The desired result
(
95
) will follow if we can
apply the dominated convergence theorem in the right-hand sides
of (
90
)
and (
91
). Using

(iv)
of
Lemma

2
and
since
\mathbb{E}(|X|)<\infty
(
\mathbb{E}(X)=0
by assumption), we have that:
for all
m>\mu
, there exist
c>0
and
N_{0}\geq 3
such that, for
all
N\geq N_{0}
, (
92
) and
(
93
) hold. Consequently we can apply the
dominated convergence theorem: using

(i)
of
Lemma

3
with

\phi_{1}(0)=\mathbb{E}(X)=0
and the fact that
\phi
is bounded by
1
yields
(
95
). Now considering the case
where
\mathbb{E}(X)\neq 0
, we get from the first limit in
(
95
) that

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X}_{N}-\mathbb{E}(X)}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)=0

and the first desired result follows using
(vii)
of
Proposition

5
.
As for the second one, applying the second limit in
(
95
) with
X
and
X_{i}

replaced by
X^{c}=X-\mathbb{E}(X)
and
X_{i}^{c}=X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X)
for all
i=1\ldots N
yields

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X^{c}}^{(i\neq j)}_{N}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)=0.

Under the present assumptions, we can apply

(ii)
of

Lemma

4
and, in particular
Identity (
89
), which, inserted in the
previous limit, gives

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\left[\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X}^{(i\neq j)}_{N}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)-2\mathbb{E}(X)\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X}_{N}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)+\mathbb{E}(X)^{2}\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{1}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)\right]=0

The limits of
\mathbb{E}\left({\overline{X}_{N}}/{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)
and

\mathbb{E}\left({\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{-\mu}}\right)
as
N\to\infty
have been
established previously to be
{\mathbb{E}(X)}/{\left(\mathbb{E}(W)\right)^{\mu}}
and

\left(\mathbb{E}(W)\right)^{-\mu}
, and this leads to the second desired limit.

(iii)

Proof of
(iii)
.
Let us define

I_{N}:=N\,\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X}_{N}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)+\mathbb{E}\left(X\,W\right)\left(\frac{N}{N-1}\right)^{\mu+1}\,\frac{\Gamma(\mu+1)}{\Gamma(\mu)}\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{1}{\left(\overline{W}_{N-1}\right)^{\mu+1}}\right)\;.

(96)

By
(ii)
with
X=1
, the second term in the right-hand side of (
96
) converges to

\mathbb{E}\left(X\,W\right)\frac{\Gamma(\mu+1)}{\Gamma(\mu)}\frac{1}{\left(\mathbb{E}(W)\right)^{\mu+1}}=\frac{\mu\;\mathbb{E}\left(X\,W\right)}{\left(\mathbb{E}(W)\right)^{\mu+1}}\;,

where we used the well known identity of the Euler Gamma
function. Now using (
84
) with
N
replaced by
N-1
and
\mu
replaced by
\mu+1
, we have: for all
N\geq 2
,

\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{1}{\left(\overline{W}_{N-1}\right)^{\mu+1}}\right)=\frac{1}{\Gamma(\mu+1)}\,\int_{0}^{\infty}u^{\mu}\left(\phi\left(\frac{u}{N-1}\right)\right)^{N-1}\;\mathrm{d}u

and by pairing it up with
(ii)
of
Lemma

4
(
\mathbb{E}(|X|)<\infty
since
\mathbb{E}(X)=0
), we get that for
N
large enough

I_{N}=N\,\left(\frac{N}{N-1}\right)^{\mu}\,\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{u^{\mu-1}}{\Gamma(\mu)}\,\left(\phi_{1}\left(\frac{u}{N-1}\right)+\frac{u\,\mathbb{E}\left(X\,W\right)}{N-1}\right)\;\left(\phi\left(\frac{u}{N-1}\right)\right)^{N-1}\;\mathrm{d}u\;.

Therefore, to conclude the proof of
(iii)
, it remains to show that
I_{N}
converges to zero as
N\to\infty
, which is implied by

\lim_{N\to\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{u^{\mu-1}}{\Gamma(\mu)}\,\left\{N\,\left(\phi_{1}\left(\frac{u}{N-1}\right)+\frac{u\,\mathbb{E}\left(X\,W\right)}{N-1}\right)\right\}\;\left(\phi\left(\frac{u}{N-1}\right)\right)^{N-1}\;\mathrm{d}u=0\;.

(97)

Using
(iv)
of
Lemma

2
and the first claim in
(ii)
of
Lemma

3
, we get that the absolute value of the integrand
in (
97
) is bounded by an
integrable function for
N
large enough, therefore the dominated convergence theorem
applies. Furthermore, using
(i)
of
Lemma

2
and the second claim in
(ii)

of
Lemma

3
, the point-wise limit of the integrand is zero.
Thus (
97
) follows from the
dominated convergence convergence theorem and the proof of
(iii)
is concluded.

(iv)

Proof of
(iv)
. Using the
definitions introduced in (
75
), we have

\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\left(\overline{X}_{N}\right)^{2}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)=\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\left(\overline{X^{2}}_{N}\right)}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)+\frac{N(N-1)}{N^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X}^{(i\neq j)}_{N}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)\;.

Under the assumption
\mathbb{E}(|X|^{2})<\infty
,
(ii)
applies to both
expectations in the right-hand side (with
X
and
X_{i}
replaced by their squares for the first
one) and yields the result.

(v)

Proof of
(v)
.
Let

0<\delta<1
. Again we can
apply the identities of
(ii)
in

Lemma

4
for
N
large enough. Here we
pick the equivalent form (
91
)
to write, for
N
large enough,

\displaystyle N^{\delta}\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X}_{N}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)=\left(\frac{N}{N-1}\right)^{\mu+\delta}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{u^{\mu-1}}{\Gamma(\mu)}(N-1)^{\delta}\,\phi_{1}\left(\frac{u}{N-1}\right)\;\left(\phi\left(\frac{u}{N-1}\right)\right)^{N-1}\mathrm{d}u.

The first claim then follows from the dominated convergence theorem
since
(iv)
of

Lemma

2
paired up with

(iii)
of
Lemma

3

yield: there exist
c>0
and
N_{0}\geq 3
such that, for all
N\geq N_{0}
,

\displaystyle\left|{u^{\mu-1}}(N-1)^{\delta}\,\phi_{1}\left(\frac{u}{N-1}\right)\;\left(\phi\left(\frac{u}{N-1}\right)\right)^{N-1}\right|\leq u^{\mu+\delta-1}\mathbb{E}(|X|{W}^{\delta})\left[1\wedge\left(c\,u^{-\mu-\delta-1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\text{and}\quad\lim_{N\to\infty}\left|{u^{\mu-1}}(N-1)^{\delta}\,\phi_{1}\left(\frac{u}{N-1}\right)\;\left(\phi\left(\frac{u}{N-1}\right)\right)^{N-1}\right|=0,

where we have also used the fact that
\phi
is bounded by
1
.

Moving on to the second claim where
\delta=1/2
and
\mathbb{E}(|X|^{2})<\infty
, we observe that

N\,\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\left(\overline{X}_{N}\right)^{2}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)=\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X^{2}}_{N}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)+(N-1)\,\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X}^{(i\neq j)}_{N}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)\;,

where
\overline{X^{2}}_{N}
and
\overline{X}^{(i\neq j)}_{N}
are defined in (
75
).
By applying
(ii)
with
X
and

X_{i}
replaced by their squares the first expectation in the
right-hand side converges to the right-hand side
of (
94
). Thus the proof of (
94
) will be complete if we can show that

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}N\,\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X}^{(i\neq j)}_{N}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)=0\;.

Under our assumptions, we can apply

(ii)
of

Lemma

4
, notably giving that, the previous
display is equivalent to

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\left(\frac{N}{N-2}\right)^{\mu+1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{u^{\mu-1}}{\Gamma(\mu)}\left((N-2)^{1/2}\phi_{1}\left(\frac{u}{N-2}\right)\right)^{2}\left(\phi\left(\frac{u}{N-2}\right)\right)^{N-2}\mathrm{d}u=0\;.

Again
(iv)
of

Lemma

2
paired up with

(iii)
of
Lemma

3

(with
\delta=1/2
)
allow us to apply the
dominated convergence theorem, which yields the claimed limit.

∎

B.4
Proof of
Theorem

1

By
(ii)
of
Proposition

5
, the condition (
17
) is equivalent to the condition (
79
). Consequntly, we will use the facts established in
Proposition

6
(which relies
on (
79
)) to prove

Theorem

1
(which assumes (
17
)).

(i)

Proof of
(i)
.
This is exactly
(ii)
of

Proposition

6
.

(ii)

Proof of
(ii)
.
We first start with the general case
\mu>0
. Multiplying both sides of (
19
) by

\left(\mathbb{E}(W)\right)^{\mu}
and replacing
W/\mathbb{E}(W)
,
W_{i}/\mathbb{E}(W)
by
W
and

W_{i}
respectively, we see that we can assume

\mathbb{E}(W)=1
without loss of generality, in which
case (
19
) reads

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}N\,\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X}_{N}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)-\mathbb{E}(X)\right)=\frac{(\mu+1)\mu\,\mathbb{E}(X)}{2}\,\mathbb{V}\left(W\right)-\mu\;\mathbb{C}\mathrm{ov}\left(X,W\right)\;.

(98)

Under our assumptions and since
\mathbb{E}(W)=1
, we can apply
(i)
and
(iii)
of
Proposition

6
, so that

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}N\,\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}(X)}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}-\mathbb{E}(X)\right)=\frac{(\mu+1)\mu\,\mathbb{E}(X)}{2}\,\mathbb{V}\left(W\right)\;,

(99)

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}N\,\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X^{c}}_{N}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\right)=-\mu\;\mathbb{C}\mathrm{ov}\left(X,W\right)\;,

(100)

where
\overline{X^{c}}_{N}
is defined in (
76
). Observing that

\frac{\overline{X^{c}}_{N}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}=\frac{\overline{X}_{N}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}-\frac{\mathbb{E}(X)}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{\mu}}\;,

we see that (
98
) is obtained by
summing (
99
) and (
100
).

Moving on to the case
\mu=1
, we can assume once again that
\mathbb{E}(W)=1
without loss of generality, in which
case (
20
) becomes

\lim_{N\to\infty}N\,\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X}_{N}}{\overline{W}_{N}}\right)-\mathbb{E}(X)\right)=\mathbb{E}\left(W\left[\mathbb{E}(X)\,W-X\right]\right)\;.

(101)

Now replacing
X
and
X_{i}
by
\left(X-\mathbb{E}(X)W\right)
and

\left(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X)W_{i}\right)
, respectively, we can further assume that

\mathbb{E}(X)=0
and (
101
) now reads

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}N\,\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X}_{N}}{\overline{W}_{N}}\right)=-\mathbb{E}\left(X\,W\right)\;,

under the assumptions
\mathbb{E}(W)=1
,
\mathbb{E}(X)=0
and
\mathbb{E}(|X|\,W)<\infty
, which follows directly from an application of
(iii)
in
Proposition

6
with
\mu=1
.

(iii)

Proof of
(iii)
.

By Minkowski’s inequality, it holds that

\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{V}\left(\frac{\overline{X}_{N}}{(\overline{W}_{N})^{\mu}}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq{\left(\mathbb{V}\left(\frac{\overline{X}_{N}-\mathbb{E}(X)}{(\overline{W}_{N})^{\mu}}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left|\mathbb{E}(X)\right|\,\left(\mathbb{V}\left(\frac{1}{(\overline{W}_{N})^{\mu}}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq{\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\left(\overline{X^{c}}_{N}\right)^{2}}{(\overline{W}_{N})^{2\mu}}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left|\mathbb{E}(X)\right|\,\left(\mathbb{V}\left(\frac{1}{(\overline{W}_{N})^{\mu}}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\;.

The variance in the second term of the sum in the last right-hand side converges to
0
by applying
(vii)
of

Proposition

5
twice. As for the first term, it
converges to zero by applying
(iv)
of

Proposition

6
(with
\mu
,
X_{i}
and
X

replaced by
2\mu
,
X^{c}
and
X_{i}^{c}
), which concludes the proof of
(iii)
.

(iv)

Proof of
(iv)
.
We start with the general case
\mu>0
. First observe that we can assume that
\mathbb{E}(W)=1
again without loss of generality. For all
N\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,

\mathbb{V}\left(\frac{\overline{X}_{N}}{(\overline{W}_{N})^{\mu}}\right)=V_{N}^{(1)}+\left(\mathbb{E}(X)\right)^{2}\,V_{N}^{(2)}+2\,\mathbb{E}(X)\,C_{N}\;,

(102)

where

\displaystyle V_{N}^{(1)}:=\mathbb{V}\left(\frac{\overline{X^{c}}_{N}}{(\overline{W}_{N})^{\mu}}\right)\;,\quad V_{N}^{(2)}:=\mathbb{V}\left(\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{-\mu}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad C_{N}:=\mathbb{C}\mathrm{ov}\left(\frac{\overline{X^{c}}_{N}}{(\overline{W}_{N})^{\mu}},\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{-\mu}\right)\;.

We treat each term separately.

•

First variance term:
V_{N}^{(1)}
. Note that
\mathbb{E}(W^{2})<\infty
and

\mathbb{E}(|X|^{2})<\infty
imply that
\mathbb{E}(|X|\,W^{1/2})
and

\mathbb{E}(|X|\,W)
are finite. We can thus
apply (
94
)
and
(iii)

of
Proposition

6
(with
\mu
,
X_{i}
and
X

replaced by
2\mu
,
X_{i}^{c}
and
X^{c}
), which gives us the asymptotic
behavior of the two expectations in the expression

V_{N}^{(1)}=\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\left(\overline{X^{c}}_{N}\right)^{2}}{(\overline{W}_{N})^{2\mu}}\right)-\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X^{c}}_{N}}{(\overline{W}_{N})^{\mu}}\right)\right)^{2}\;.

We obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}N\,V_{N}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{V}\left(X\right)\;.

(103)

•

Second variance term:
V_{N}^{(2)}
. For all
N\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,

\mathbb{V}\left(\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{-\mu}\right)=\left\{\mathbb{E}\left(\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{-2\mu}\right)-1\right\}-\left\{\left(1+\left[\mathbb{E}\left(\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{-\mu}\right)-1\right]\right)^{2}-1\right\}\;,

Applying
(i)
of

Proposition

6
to get the asymptotic behavior of
the expectations in the right-hand side, we get that

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}N\,\mathbb{V}\left(\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{-\mu}\right)

\displaystyle=(2\mu+1)\,\mu\,\mathbb{V}\left(W\right)-(\mu+1)\,\mu\,\mathbb{V}\left(W\right)

\displaystyle=\mu^{2}\,\mathbb{V}\left(W\right)\;.

(104)

•

Covariance term:
C_{N}
.

C_{N}=\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X^{c}}_{N}}{(\overline{W}_{N})^{2\mu}}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X^{c}}_{N}}{(\overline{W}_{N})^{\mu}}\right)\,\mathbb{E}\left(\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)^{-\mu}\right)\;.

Applying
(iii)

of
Proposition

6
twice, once
with
\mu
replaced by
2\mu
and once again without changing
\mu
, along with

(vii)
of
Proposition

5
, we get that

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}N\,C_{N}

\displaystyle=-2\mu\;\mathbb{C}\mathrm{ov}\left(X,W\right)+\mu\;\mathbb{C}\mathrm{ov}\left(X,W\right)=-\mu\;\mathbb{C}\mathrm{ov}\left(X,W\right)\;.

Combining (
102
) with (
103
), (
104
) and the above limit, we obtain that

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}N\,\mathbb{V}\left(\frac{\overline{X}_{N}}{(\overline{W}_{N})^{\mu}}\right)=\mathbb{V}\left(X\right)+\left(\mathbb{E}(X)\right)^{2}\,\mu^{2}\,\mathbb{V}\left(W\right)-2\,\mathbb{E}(X)\,\mu\;\mathbb{C}\mathrm{ov}\left(X,W\right)\;.

This is exactly the desired result
(iv)
in the case
\mathbb{E}(W)=1
, and the proof is completed for a general
\mu>0
.

Moving on to the case
\mu=1
, we can assume once more that
\mathbb{E}(W)=1
without loss of generality, in which case (
23
) reads

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}N\,\mathbb{V}\left(\frac{\overline{X}_{N}}{{\overline{W}_{N}}}\right)=\mathbb{V}\left(X-\mathbb{E}(X)W\right)\;.

(105)

Replacing
X
and
X_{i}
by
(X-\mathbb{E}(X)W)
and
(X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(X)W_{i})
,
respectively, we can further assume that
\mathbb{E}(X)=0
,

\mathbb{E}(X^{2})<\infty
(transposing the original

\mathbb{E}(\left|\mathbb{E}(W)X-\mathbb{E}(X)W\right|^{2})<\infty
to the new definition of
X

when
\mathbb{E}(W)=1
) and (
105
) reads

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}N\,\mathbb{V}\left(\frac{\overline{X}_{N}}{{\overline{W}_{N}}}\right)=\mathbb{V}\left(X\right)\;.

(106)

Now using that
\mathbb{E}(|X|^{2})<\infty
and
\mathbb{E}(W)=1
, which implies

\mathbb{E}(|X|\sqrt{W})<\infty
, we can apply the two assertions in

(v)
of
Proposition

6

with
\mu=1,2
and
\delta=1/2
so that

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}N\left(\,\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X}_{N}}{\left(\overline{W}_{N}\right)}\right)\right)^{2}=0\quad\text{and}\quad\lim_{N\to\infty}N\,\mathbb{E}\left(\left(\frac{\overline{X}_{N}}{\overline{W}_{N}}\right)^{2}\right)={\mathbb{V}\left(X\right)}.

This yields (
106
) and the
proof is completed.

B.5
Related works for
Theorem

1

(i)

Deligiannidis et al., 2025
concurrently established a result that is comparable to (
20
): their analysis in
Deligiannidis et al., 2025
, Theorem 2.1
focused on the self-normalized importance sampling estimator, which corresponds to taking
(X,W)=(f(Z)\omega(Z),\omega(Z))
with
Z\sim q
, where
\omega=\pi/q
is the importance weight,
\pi
is the target distribution,
q
the proposal distribution and
f
is a function of interest. Letting
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{N}
be i.i.d. random variables with the same distribution as
Z
, (
20
) then becomes

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}N\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{N}w(Z_{i})f(Z_{i})}{\sum_{i=1}^{N}w(Z_{i})}\right)-\mathbb{E}(w(Z)f(Z))\right)=\mathbb{E}\left(w(Z)^{2}\left[f(Z)-\mathbb{E}(w(Z)f(Z))\right]\right),

which is exactly the result obtained in
Deligiannidis et al., 2025
. However, their derivation relies on a distinct proof strategy that necessitates a stronger positive moment condition on
|\mathbb{E}(W)X-\mathbb{E}(X)W|W
.

(ii)

Rainforth et al., 2018
and
Daudel et al., 2023
directed their analyses exclusively towards the REP gradient estimator of the IWAE and VR-IWAE bound respectively. They showed that (
20
) and (
23
) hold for specific choices of
(X,W)
under much stringent conditions than that of
Theorem

1
. More precisely, letting
\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}
, they take

\displaystyle X=w_{\theta,\phi}(f(\varepsilon,\phi;x);x)^{1-\alpha}\partial_{\psi}\log w_{\theta,\phi}(f(\varepsilon,\phi;x);x)

\displaystyle W=w_{\theta,\phi}(f(\varepsilon,\phi;x);x)^{1-\alpha},

with
Rainforth et al., 2018
considering the case
\alpha=0
and assuming that
X
and
W
have moments of order
6
, while
Daudel et al., 2023
considers the case
\alpha\in(0,1)
and assumes moments of order
8
for
X
and
W
. The derivations in
Rainforth et al., 2018
and
Daudel et al., 2023
rely on distinct proof strategies that necessitates stronger conditions compared to those used in
Theorem

1
.

Appendix C
Deferred proofs and results for
Section

3.3

C.1
Proofs of

Theorems

2
,
3
and
4

C.1.1
Notation

Let us first introduce some notation and resulting identities that will be used for the proofs of
Theorems

2
,
3
and
4
. Letting
\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}
and with
\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)
as in (
24
), we define

X=(1-\alpha)^{-1}\partial_{\psi}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad W=\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\;,

Furthermore, let

\varepsilon_{1},\ldots,\varepsilon_{N}
be i.i.d. copies of

\varepsilon
, and define

X_{i}
and

W_{i}
for all
i=1,2,\ldots
as
X
and
W
but with
\varepsilon_{i}

replacing
\varepsilon
. Further
denote
\overline{X}_{N}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}X_{i}
and

\overline{W}_{N}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}W_{i}
for all
N\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
.
Then, using the above
definitions in (
14
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\psi\text{-}g_{1,N}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)\right)=\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X}_{N}}{\overline{W}_{N}}\right)\;,

(107)

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}(\psi\text{-}g_{1,N}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x))=\mathbb{V}\left(\frac{\overline{X}_{N}}{\overline{W}_{N}}\right)\;,

(108)

Remark 3

We note that with these definitions,
(A5)
means
that (
17
) holds with

\eta=\delta/(1-\alpha)
,
\mathbb{V}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\right)<\infty
that
\mathbb{E}\left(W^{2}\right)<\infty
,
\mathbb{V}\left({\partial_{\psi}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\right)}\right)<\infty
that
\mathbb{E}\left(\left|X\right|^{2}\right)<\infty
and
(A

df

REP

)
implies
\mathbb{E}\left(\left|X\right|\,W^{\ell-1}\right)<\infty
for
\ell=0,1,2
.

Remark 4

The above quantities are defined for
0\leq\alpha<1
. However, the
definition of
W
boils down for
\alpha=1
to
W=1
, hence

\overline{W}_{N}=1
for all
N\geq 1
. As for
X
, since it holds
for all
\alpha\in[0,1)
that

X=W\partial_{\psi}\left(\log\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)\right)
, it
becomes
X=\partial_{\psi}\left(\log\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)\right)
for

\alpha=1
. In this case, explicit formulas hold
of (
107
) and (
108
), namely,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\psi\text{-}g_{1,N}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)\right)=\mathbb{E}\left(\partial_{\psi}\left(\log\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)\right)\right)=\partial_{\psi}\mathrm{ELBO}(\theta,\phi;x)\;,

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}(\psi\text{-}g_{1,N}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x))=\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{V}\left(\partial_{\psi}\left(\log\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)\right)\right)\;.

where the second equality in the first displayed line follows from

(A

df

REP

)
, which for
\alpha=1
takes the form

\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\sup_{(\theta^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}}\left|\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\left(\log\tilde{w}_{\theta^{\prime},\phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)\right)\right|\right)<\infty\;.

Thus the results of
Theorems

2
and
3
hold for
\alpha=1
in a non-asymptotic way,
namely, with all
o
-terms replaced by zeros,

\mathrm{VR}^{(1)}(\theta,\phi;x)=\mathrm{ELBO}(\theta,\phi;x)
,

\psi\text{-}V^{(1,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)=\mathbb{V}\left(\partial_{\psi}\left(\log\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)\right)\right)

and
\partial_{\psi}[\gamma^{(1)}(\theta,\phi;x)^{2}]=0
.

C.1.2
Proof of
Theorem

2

Under
(A

df

REP

)
, it holds that

\displaystyle\partial_{\psi}\ell^{(\alpha)}_{N}(\theta,\phi;x)=\mathbb{E}(\psi\text{-}g_{M,N}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x))

(109)

\displaystyle\partial_{\psi}\mathrm{VR}^{(\alpha)}(\theta,\phi;x)=\frac{\mathbb{E}(X)}{\mathbb{E}(W)}

(110)

\displaystyle\partial_{\psi}[\gamma^{(\alpha)}(\theta,\phi;x)^{2}]=2\,\frac{\mathbb{E}\left(W\left[\mathbb{E}(W)\,X-\mathbb{E}(X)\,W\right]\right)}{\left(\mathbb{E}(W)\right)^{3}},

(111)

which follows from
Proposition

2
, the definitions of
Section

C.1.1
,
(
12
), (
13
), (
8
) and (
56
)–(
58
), as they imply

\displaystyle\ell^{(\alpha)}_{N}(\theta,\phi;x)=\frac{1}{1-\alpha}\,\mathbb{E}\left(\log\overline{W}_{N}\right)\;,

\displaystyle\mathrm{VR}^{(\alpha)}(\theta,\phi;x)=\frac{1}{1-\alpha}\,\log\mathbb{E}\left(W\right)\;,

\displaystyle\gamma^{(\alpha)}(\theta,\phi;x)^{2}=\frac{1}{1-\alpha}\,\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}(W^{2})}{\mathbb{E}(W)^{2}}-1\right)\;,

\displaystyle\partial_{\psi}\mathbb{E}(W)=(1-\alpha)\,\mathbb{E}(X)\;,

(112)

\displaystyle\partial_{\psi}\mathbb{E}\left(W^{2}\right)=2\,(1-\alpha)\,\mathbb{E}\left(X\,W\right)\;,

\displaystyle\partial_{\psi}\mathbb{E}\left(\log\overline{W}_{n}\right)=(1-\alpha)\,\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\overline{X}_{n}}{\overline{W}_{n}}\right)\;.

We can conclude by using
Remark

3
and the interchanges of the derivative and expectation signs obtained above under
(A

df

REP

)
to rewrite the asymptotic results
(i)
and the second part of
(ii)
from
Theorem

1
into the convenient formulations (
26
) and (
27
) respectively.

C.1.3
Proof of
Theorem

3

Note that the conditions of
Theorem

3
are stronger
than those required in
Theorem

2
. We thus already
have that (
27
) holds as
N\to\infty
,
which gives that
|\mathbb{E}(\psi\text{-}g_{M,N}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x))|
behaves as the numerator
of the r.h.s of (
28
). To conclude the proof, it remains to study the asymptotic behavior of
\mathbb{V}(\psi\text{-}g_{M,N}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x))
as

N\to\infty
. As in the proof of
Theorem

2
, we
first note that, following

Remark

3
, the assumptions of
Theorem

3

imply

(W1)

There exists
\eta>0
such that (
17
)
holds and
\mathbb{E}(|X|)
,
\mathbb{E}(W)
and

\mathbb{E}\left(\left|\mathbb{E}(W)X-\mathbb{E}(X)W\right|^{2}\right)
are all finite.

Then the claimed asymptotic behavior of the variance is obtained in
two steps: (i) we show a first asymptotic behavior for

\mathbb{V}(\psi\text{-}g_{1,N}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x))
as
N\to\infty
which holds
under
(W1)
and (ii) we show how the
conclusions of
Proposition

2
allow us to
express this asymptotic result under the convenient formulation
(
29
).

First step.

Under
(W1)
, we can apply the second
part of Assertion
(iv)
of

Theorem

1
. Using (
23
)
and (
108
) we have that, as
N\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}(\psi\text{-}g_{1,N}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\frac{\mathbb{V}\left(\mathbb{E}(W)X-\mathbb{E}(X)W\right)}{\left(\mathbb{E}(W)\right)^{4}}+o\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{V}\left({\frac{X}{\mathbb{E}(W)}-W\frac{\mathbb{E}(X)}{\mathbb{E}(W)^{2}}}\right)+o\left(\frac{1}{N}\right).

Second step.
We prove that, under
(A

df

REP

)
, the constant defined
in (
29
) reads
