\displaystyle\psi\text{-}V^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)=\mathbb{V}\left({\frac{X}{\mathbb{E}(W)}-W\frac{\mathbb{E}(X)}{\mathbb{E}(W)^{2}}}\right)\;.

(113)

By using the definitions of
X
and
W
in

Section

C.1.1
and (
56
) of
Proposition

2
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{\psi}\left(\frac{W}{\mathbb{E}(W)}\right)=\frac{\partial_{\psi}W}{\mathbb{E}(W)}-W\frac{\partial_{\psi}\mathbb{E}(W)}{(\mathbb{E}(W))^{2}}=(1-\alpha)\left({\frac{X}{\mathbb{E}(W)}-W\frac{\mathbb{E}(X)}{\mathbb{E}(W)^{2}}}\right)

meaning that the two variables in the variances of the right-hand sides of (
29
)
and (
113
) coincide up to the
(1-\alpha)
multiplicative term, which is squared when put in front of the variance. The
identity (
113
) follows.

Remark 5

As a byproduct of the proof of
Section

C.1.3
, we have established (
113
), that is using the definition of
X
and
W
in
Section

C.1.1
,

\displaystyle\psi\text{-}V^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(1-\alpha)^{2}}\mathbb{V}\left({\frac{\partial_{\psi}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha})}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha})}-\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\mathbb{E}(\partial_{\psi}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}))}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha})^{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{V}\left({\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha})}\left(\partial_{\psi}(\log\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x))-\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha})}\partial_{\psi}(\log\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x))\right)\right)}\right).

Now considering the case
p_{\theta}(\cdot|x)=q_{\phi}(\cdot|x)
in the above and using

\displaystyle\partial_{\psi}\log w_{\theta,\phi}(f(\varepsilon,\phi;x);x)=\left.\left[\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\log w_{\theta,\phi}(f(\varepsilon,\phi^{\prime};x);x)-\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\log q_{\phi^{\prime}}(f(\varepsilon,\phi;x)|x)\right]\right|_{\phi^{\prime}=\phi},

(114)

it follows that

\displaystyle\psi\text{-}V^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)=\mathbb{V}\left(\left.\left[\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\log q_{\phi^{\prime}}(f(\varepsilon,\phi;x)|x)\right]\right|_{\phi^{\prime}=\phi}\right)\quad\mbox{when $p_{\theta}(\cdot|x)=q_{\phi}(\cdot|x)$}.

(115)

C.1.4
Proof of
Theorem

4

We first introduce some additional notation for the DREP
estimators. Namely, we set

\displaystyle X^{(1)}=\frac{1}{1-\alpha}\left.\left[\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\right)\right]\right|_{\phi^{\prime}=\phi}=\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\left.\left[\partial_{\psi}\log\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)\right]\right|_{\phi^{\prime}=\phi}

\displaystyle X^{(2)}=\frac{1}{2(1-\alpha)}\left.\left[\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)^{2(1-\alpha)}\right)\right]\right|_{\phi^{\prime}=\phi}=\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}X^{(1)}=W\,X^{(1)}\;.

Then,

,
\mathbb{V}\left({\left.\left[\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\right)\right]\right|_{\phi^{\prime}=\phi}}\right)<\infty
that

\mathbb{E}\left(|X^{(1)}|^{2}\right)<\infty
and

:

As in

(116)

and we further define

\displaystyle\text{and}\quad\psi\text{-}V^{(\alpha,\mathrm{DREP})}_{2}(\theta,\phi;x)=

\displaystyle(1-\alpha)^{2}\,\mathbb{V}\left({\frac{X^{(2)}}{\mathbb{E}(W)^{2}}-2W\frac{\mathbb{E}(X^{(2)})}{\mathbb{E}(W)^{3}}}\right)\;.

(121)

As for the proofs of

df

Remark 6

As in
Remark

4
for the REP estimator, let us briefly examine the
case
\alpha=1
. In this case we get

\psi\text{-}g_{1,N}^{(1,\mathrm{DREP})}(\theta,\phi;x)=\overline{X}^{(1)}_{N}
with
X^{(1)}=\left.\left[\partial_{\psi}\log\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)\right]\right|_{\phi^{\prime}=\phi}
. When the DREP estimator is unbiased
(under
(A

df

DREP

)
adapted to
\alpha=1
), we further have
that
\mathbb{E}\left(X^{(1)}\right)=\partial_{\psi}\mathrm{ELBO}(\theta,\phi;x)
, so that, for all
N\geq 1
,

\mathrm{SNR}[\psi\text{-}g_{1,N}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{DREP})}(\theta,\phi;x)]=\sqrt{N}\penalty\ \frac{\left|\partial_{\psi}\mathrm{ELBO}(\theta,\phi;x)\right|}{\sqrt{\psi\text{-}V^{(1,\mathrm{DREP})}(\theta,\phi;x)}}\;,

with
\psi\text{-}V^{(1,\mathrm{DREP})}(\theta,\phi;x)=\mathbb{V}\left(\left.\left[\partial_{\psi}\log\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)\right]\right|_{\phi^{\prime}=\phi}\right)
. This extends the asymptotic
formula (
31
) obtained for
\alpha\in(0,1)
to the case
\alpha=1
.

C.2
Proof of examples

Before providing the proofs for
Examples

1
and
2
, we first recall below the two settings considered in these two examples alongside with some known results taken from
Daudel et al., 2023
.

Example 4

(Known results from
Daudel et al., 2023
associated to
Example

1
)

Let
\theta,\phi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Set
p_{\theta}(z|x)=\mathcal{N}(z;\theta,\boldsymbol{I}_{d})
and
q_{\phi}(z|x)=\mathcal{N}(z;\phi,\boldsymbol{I}_{d})
, where
\boldsymbol{I}_{d}
is the
d
-dimensional identity matrix. Then, denoting the Euclidean norm of a finite dimensional vector
x
with real
entries by

\left\|x\right\|
and its associated inner product by
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{VR}^{(\alpha)}(\theta,\phi;x)=\ell(\theta;x)-\frac{{\alpha}\|\theta-\phi\|^{2}}{2}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\gamma^{(\alpha)}(\theta,\phi;x)^{2}=\frac{\exp\left((1-\alpha)^{2}\|\theta-\phi\|^{2}\right)-1}{1-\alpha}.

In addition, it holds that

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{p_{\theta}(z|x)}{q_{\phi}(z|x)}\right)=-\frac{\|\theta-\phi\|^{2}}{2}-\|\theta-\phi\|S,\quad S=\frac{\langle z-\phi,\phi-\theta\rangle}{\|\theta-\phi\|},\quad z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

For
\phi=\theta+\boldsymbol{u}_{d}
, we have

\log\left(\frac{p_{\theta}(Z|x)}{q_{\phi}(Z|x)}\right)=-\frac{d}{2}-\sqrt{d}{S},\quad S\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1).

Example 5

(Known results from
Daudel et al., 2023
associated to
Example

2
)

Let
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\phi=(\tilde{a},\tilde{b})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
A=\mathrm{diag}(\tilde{a})
. Set
p_{\theta}(z)=\mathcal{N}(z;\theta,\boldsymbol{I}_{d})
,
p_{\theta}(x|z)=\mathcal{N}(x;z,\boldsymbol{I}_{d})
and
q_{\phi}(z|x)=\mathcal{N}(z;Ax+\tilde{b},2/3\penalty\ \boldsymbol{I}_{d})

as in
Rainforth et al., 2018
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathrm{VR}^{(\alpha)}(\theta,\phi;x)=\ell(\theta;x)+\frac{d}{2}\left(\log\left(\frac{4}{3}\right)+\frac{1}{1-\alpha}\log\left(\frac{3}{4-\alpha}\right)\right)-\frac{3\alpha}{4-\alpha}\Big\|Ax+\tilde{b}-\frac{\theta+x}{2}\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\gamma^{(\alpha)}(\theta,\phi;x)^{2}=\frac{1}{1-\alpha}\left((4-\alpha)^{d}(15-6\alpha)^{-\frac{d}{2}}\exp\left(\frac{24(1-\alpha)^{2}}{(5-2\alpha)(4-\alpha)}\Big\|Ax+\tilde{b}-\frac{\theta+x}{2}\Big\|^{2}\right)-1\right).

In addition, it holds that

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{p_{\theta}(Z|x)}{q_{\phi}(Z|x)}\right)=-\log\mathbb{E}(\exp(-\sigma\sqrt{d}S))-\sigma\sqrt{d}S,\quad S=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\frac{\xi_{j}}{\sigma\sqrt{d}}

with
\sigma^{2}=\mathbb{V}(\xi_{1})={1}/{18}+{8\lambda^{2}}/{3}<\infty
and
\xi_{j}=y_{j}^{2}/4-2\lambda y_{j}-1/6
for all
j=1\ldots d
, where
\lambda=\big\|\frac{\theta+x}{2}-Ax-\tilde{b}\big\|/\sqrt{d}
,
y=(y_{1},\ldots,y_{d})\sim\mathcal{N}(0,2/3\boldsymbol{I}_{d})
and
\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{d}
are centered i.i.d. random variables which are absolutely continuous with respect to the Lebesgue measure.

We next review the assumptions that need to be checked to obtain
Examples

1
and
2
.

Checking assumptions

First note that (i) the assumption
(A3)

for both

and

,
\mathbb{V}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\right)<\infty

,

df

,

)

(136)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\sup_{(\theta^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}}\left|\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\log\tilde{w}_{\theta^{\prime},\phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)\right|^{p}\right)<\infty,

where
\psi
is a given component of
(\theta,\phi)
.

\mathbb{V}\left({[\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)^{2(1-\alpha)})]|_{\phi^{\prime}=\phi}}\right)<\infty

(141)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim q_{\phi}(\cdot|x)}\left(\sup_{(\theta,\phi^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}}\left|\partial_{\psi}\log q_{\phi^{\prime}}(Z|x)\right|^{p}\right)<\infty,

(142)

where
\psi
is a given component of

\phi
.

and

C.2.2
Proof of
Example

1

Recall that in this setting
\psi
is a component of
\phi
, say
\phi_{k}
for some all
k=1\ldots d
,
p_{\theta}(z|x)=\mathcal{N}(z;\theta,\boldsymbol{I}_{d})
and

q_{\phi}(z|x)=\mathcal{N}(z;\phi,\boldsymbol{I}_{d})
, where

\boldsymbol{I}_{d}
denotes the
d
-dimensional identity matrix.

•

Checking
(A5)
,
\mathbb{V}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\right)<\infty
,
\mathbb{V}\left({\partial_{\psi}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\right)}\right)<\infty

(A

df

REP

)
,
\mathbb{V}\left({[\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)^{2(1-\alpha)})]|_{\phi^{\prime}=\phi}}\right)<\infty
,
\mathbb{V}\left({\left.\left[\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\right)\right]\right|_{\phi^{\prime}=\phi}}\right)<\infty
and
(A

df

DREP

)
.
Let us prove (
135
)-(
142
). To this end, observe that

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{p_{\theta}(z|x)}{q_{\phi}(z|x)}\right)=-\frac{\|\theta-\phi\|^{2}}{2}-\langle z-\phi,\phi-\theta\rangle,\quad z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

(143)

Since
\langle Z-\phi,\phi-\theta\rangle
is a Gaussian
variable if
Z\sim q_{\phi}(\cdot|x)
, it admits exponential moments of
any exponent and we have that (
135
) holds. We
further get from (
143
) that

\log\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)=c_{0}(\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime})+\langle\varepsilon,\phi-\theta\rangle
for some continuous function
c_{0}
defined on

\Theta\times\Phi^{2}
. Conditions (
136
)–(
142
) follow using that
\varepsilon
is a
Gaussian variable.

•

Closed-form expressions for the quantities
appearing in
Theorems

2
,
3
and
4
.
First recall from
Example

4
that

\displaystyle\mathrm{VR}^{(\alpha)}(\theta,\phi;x)=\ell(\theta;x)-\frac{{\alpha}\|\theta-\phi\|^{2}}{2},\quad\gamma^{(\alpha)}(\theta,\phi;x)^{2}=\frac{\exp\left((1-\alpha)^{2}\|\theta-\phi\|^{2}\right)-1}{1-\alpha}\;.

(144)

Computing
\partial_{\psi}\mathrm{VR}^{(\alpha)}(\theta,\phi;x)

and
\partial_{\psi}[\gamma^{(\alpha)}(\theta,\phi;x)^{2}]
follows directly
from differentiating in (
144
) w.r.t.
\phi_{k}
:

\displaystyle\partial_{\phi_{k}}\mathrm{VR}^{(\alpha)}(\theta,\phi;x)=-\alpha(\phi_{k}-\theta_{k})

(145)

\displaystyle\partial_{\phi_{k}}[\gamma^{(\alpha)}(\theta,\phi;x)^{2}]=2{(1-\alpha)(\phi_{k}-\theta_{k})\exp\left((1-\alpha)^{2}\|\phi-\theta\|^{2}\right)}.

(146)

Furthermore, using (
143
): for all
y>0
and all
\phi^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\log\left(\left(\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x))}\right)^{y}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{y\|\theta-\phi\|^{2}}{2}-y\langle\varepsilon+\phi^{\prime}-\phi,\phi-\theta\rangle

\displaystyle=yg(\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime})-y\langle\varepsilon,\phi-\theta\rangle,

(147)

where
g(\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime})=-\frac{\|\theta-\phi\|^{2}}{2}-y\langle\phi^{\prime}-\phi,\phi-\theta\rangle
. Setting
y=1-\alpha
in (
147
), we deduce that:

\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\exp\left(-(1-\alpha)\langle\varepsilon,\phi-\theta\rangle\right)}{\mathbb{E}(\exp\left(-(1-\alpha)\langle\varepsilon,\phi-\theta\rangle\right))}=\frac{\exp\left(-(1-\alpha)\|\theta-\phi\|\tilde{S}\right)}{\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}(1-\alpha)^{2}\|\theta-\phi\|^{2}\right)}

(148)

with
\tilde{S}=\frac{\langle\varepsilon,\phi-\theta\rangle}{\|\theta-\phi\|}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. Hence,

\displaystyle\partial_{\phi_{k}}\left(\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha})}\right)=-(1-\alpha)\left(\varepsilon^{(k)}+(1-\alpha)(\phi_{k}-\theta_{k})\right)\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha})}

\displaystyle\left.\left[\partial_{\phi^{\prime}_{k}}\left(\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha})}\right)\right]\right|_{\phi^{\prime}=\phi}=0,

where
\varepsilon^{(k)}
denotes the
k
-th element of
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\phi_{k}\text{-}V^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)=\mathbb{E}\left({\left(\varepsilon^{(k)}+(1-\alpha)(\phi_{k}-\theta_{k})\right)^{2}}\left(\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha})}\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\phi_{k}\text{-}V^{(\alpha,\mathrm{DREP})}(\theta,\phi;x)=0,\quad\alpha\in(0,1).

(149)

Thus, using (
148
), we have that: for all
\psi\in\{\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{d},\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{d}\}
,

\displaystyle\phi_{k}\text{-}V^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{-(1-\alpha)^{2}\|\theta-\phi\|^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left((\varepsilon^{(k)}+(1-\alpha)(\phi_{k}-\theta_{k}))^{2}\mathrm{e}^{-2(1-\alpha)\langle\varepsilon,\phi-\theta\rangle}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{-(1-\alpha)^{2}\|\theta-\phi\|^{2}}\mathrm{e}^{2(1-\alpha)^{2}\|\theta-\phi\|^{2}}\left(1+(1-\alpha)^{2}(\phi_{k}-\theta_{k})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{e}^{(1-\alpha)^{2}\|\theta-\phi\|^{2}}\left(1+(1-\alpha)^{2}(\phi_{k}-\theta_{k})^{2}\right).

(150)

Now considering the special case
\alpha=0
, it holds that using (
114
):

\displaystyle\phi_{k}\text{-}V^{(0,\mathrm{DREP})}(\theta,\phi;x)=\mathbb{V}\left(A_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)-2\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x))}\mathbb{E}\left(A_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(\left(A_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)-2\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x))}\mathbb{E}\left(A_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)\right)\right)^{2}\right)-\mathbb{E}(A_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x))^{2}

(151)

with

A_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)=\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{2}}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x))^{2}}\left(\partial_{\phi_{k}}\log\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)+\left.\left[\partial_{\phi^{\prime}_{k}}\log q_{\phi^{\prime}}(f(\varepsilon,\phi;x)|x)\right]\right|_{\phi^{\prime}=\phi}\right).

Since
\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x))=\exp(\ell(\theta;x))
and thus does not depend on
\phi_{k}
, we deduce that

\displaystyle A_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\phi_{k}}\left(\left(\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x))}\right)^{2}\right)+\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{2}}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x))^{2}}\left.\left[\partial_{\phi^{\prime}_{k}}\log q_{\phi^{\prime}}(f(\varepsilon,\phi;x)|x)\right]\right|_{\phi^{\prime}=\phi}.

(152)

Using (
148
) with
\alpha=0
, taking the square and differentiating, we have that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\phi_{k}}\left(\left(\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x))}\right)^{2}\right)=-\left(\varepsilon^{(k)}+\phi_{k}-\theta_{k}\right)\left(\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x))}\right)^{2}.

Furthermore,
\left.\left[\partial_{\phi^{\prime}_{k}}\log q_{\phi^{\prime}}(f(\varepsilon,\phi;x)|x)\right]\right|_{\phi^{\prime}=\phi}=\left.\left[\varepsilon^{(k)}+\phi-\phi^{\prime}\right]\right|_{\phi^{\prime}=\phi}=\varepsilon^{(k)}
and thus

\displaystyle A_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)=(\theta_{k}-\phi_{k})\left(\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x))}\right)^{2}.

Consequently, since for all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\left(\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x))}\right)^{\ell}\right)=\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\ell(\ell-1)}{2}\|\theta-\phi\|^{2}},

(153)

we obtain that

\displaystyle\phi_{k}\text{-}V^{(0,\mathrm{DREP})}(\theta,\phi;x)

\displaystyle=(\theta_{k}-\phi_{k})^{2}\mathrm{e}^{2\|\theta-\phi\|^{2}}\;\left(\mathrm{e}^{4\|\theta-\phi\|^{2}}-4\mathrm{e}^{2\|\theta-\phi\|^{2}}+4\mathrm{e}^{\|\theta-\phi\|^{2}}-1\right).

(154)

Lastly, we obtain the desired expressions in
Example

1
by
taking
\theta-\phi=\epsilon\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{d}
with

\epsilon>0
in (
145
),
(
146
), (
149
),
(
150
) and applying

Theorems

2
,
3
and
4
.

C.2.3
Proof of
Example

2

Recall that in this setting
\psi
is a component of
(\theta,\tilde{b})
, say
\theta_{k}
or
\tilde{b}_{k}
for some
k=1\ldots d
,
p_{\theta}(z)=\mathcal{N}(z;\theta,\boldsymbol{I}_{d})
,

p_{\theta}(x|z)=\mathcal{N}(x;z,\boldsymbol{I}_{d})
and

q_{\phi}(z|x)=\mathcal{N}(z;Ax+\tilde{b},2/3\penalty\ \boldsymbol{I}_{d})
with

A=\mathrm{diag}(\tilde{a})
and
\tilde{a}=(\tilde{a}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{a}_{d})
.
We thus have that

\displaystyle p_{\theta}(x,z)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\left(\|z-\theta\|^{2}+\|z-x\|^{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle p_{\theta}(x)=\int p_{\theta}(x,z)\mathrm{d}z=\mathcal{N}(x;\theta,2\boldsymbol{I}_{d}),

(155)

\displaystyle p_{\theta}(z|x)=\mathcal{N}(z;(\theta+x)/2,1/2\penalty\ \boldsymbol{I}_{d})\;.

(156)

•

Checking
(A5)
,
\mathbb{V}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\right)<\infty
,
\mathbb{V}\left({\partial_{\psi}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\right)}\right)<\infty

(A

df

REP

)
,
\mathbb{V}\left({[\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)^{2(1-\alpha)})]|_{\phi^{\prime}=\phi}}\right)<\infty
,
\mathbb{V}\left({\left.\left[\partial_{\psi^{\prime}}\left(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}\right)\right]\right|_{\phi^{\prime}=\phi}}\right)<\infty
and
(A

df

DREP

)
.
Let us prove (
135
)-(
142
). To this end, using the densities and conditional densities above, we obtain that

\log w_{\theta,\phi}(z;x)=C_{1}(\theta,\phi;x)-\frac{1}{4}\|z\|^{2}+\langle z,\theta+x-\frac{3}{2}\left(Ax+\tilde{b}\right)\rangle\;,

where
C_{1}(\cdot;x)
is a
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}

function from
\Theta\times\Phi

to
\mathbb{R}
. For
Z\sim q_{\phi}(\cdot|x)
, it is a Gaussian vector with
scalar covariance matrix equal to
2/3
on its diagonal, and we get
that (
135
) holds for any
\eta<3
. Next, using that

f(\varepsilon,\phi^{\prime};x)=\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}\varepsilon+A^{\prime}x+\tilde{b}^{\prime}
. we have

\log\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)=C_{2}(\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime};x)-\frac{1}{6}\|\varepsilon\|^{2}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}\left\langle\varepsilon,A^{\prime}x+\tilde{b}^{\prime}-2\left(\theta+x\right)+3\left(Ax+\tilde{b}\right)\right\rangle\;,

where
C_{2}(\cdot;x)
is a
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}

function from
\Theta\times\Phi^{2}

to
\mathbb{R}
. Since

\tilde{q}=\mathcal{N}(0,\boldsymbol{I}_{d})
, we easily
get (
136
)–(
139
). Conditions (
140
)–(
142
)
are obtained similarly.

•

Closed-form expressions for the quantities appearing in
Theorems

2
,
3
and
4
.

First recall from
Example

5
that

\displaystyle\mathrm{VR}^{(\alpha)}(\theta,\phi;x)=\ell(\theta;x)+\frac{d}{2}\left(\log\left(\frac{4}{3}\right)+\frac{1}{1-\alpha}\log\left(\frac{3}{4-\alpha}\right)\right)-\frac{3\alpha}{4-\alpha}\Big\|Ax+\tilde{b}-\frac{\theta+x}{2}\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\gamma^{(\alpha)}(\theta,\phi;x)^{2}=\frac{1}{1-\alpha}\left[(4-\alpha)^{d}(15-6\alpha)^{-\frac{d}{2}}\exp\left(\frac{24(1-\alpha)^{2}}{(5-2\alpha)(4-\alpha)}\Big\|Ax+\tilde{b}-\frac{\theta+x}{2}\Big\|^{2}\right)-1\right].

Computing
\partial_{\psi}\mathrm{VR}^{(\alpha)}(\theta,\phi;x)

and
\partial_{\psi}[\gamma^{(\alpha)}(\theta,\phi;x)^{2}]
follows directly
from differentiating the equations above w.r.t.
\theta
/
\phi
paired
up with (
155
). so that

\displaystyle\partial_{\theta_{k}}\mathrm{VR}^{(\alpha)}(\theta,\phi;x)=\frac{x_{k}-\theta_{k}}{2}+\frac{3\alpha}{4-\alpha}\left(\tilde{a}_{k}x_{k}+\tilde{b}_{k}-\frac{\theta_{k}+x_{k}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tilde{b}_{k}}\mathrm{VR}^{(\alpha)}(\theta,\phi;x)=-2\cdot\frac{3\alpha}{4-\alpha}\left(\tilde{a}_{k}x_{k}+\tilde{b}_{k}-\frac{\theta_{k}+x_{k}}{2}\right)

and

\partial_{\tilde{b}_{k}}[\gamma^{(\alpha)}(\theta,\phi;x)^{2}]=2\cdot\frac{24(1-\alpha)(4-\alpha)^{d-1}}{3^{\frac{d}{2}}(5-2\alpha)^{\frac{d}{2}+1}}\exp\left(\frac{24(1-\alpha)^{2}}{(5-2\alpha)(4-\alpha)}\Big\|Ax+\tilde{b}-\frac{\theta+x}{2}\Big\|^{2}\right)\\
\times\left(\tilde{a}_{k}x_{k}+\tilde{b}_{k}-\frac{\theta_{k}+x_{k}}{2}\right).

As for computing
\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)
above, we have that, for all
\phi,\phi^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}
,

\frac{p_{\theta}(f(\varepsilon,\phi^{\prime};x)|x)}{q_{\phi}(f(\varepsilon,\phi^{\prime};x)|x)}=C\exp\left(\frac{3}{4}\|A^{\prime}x+\tilde{b}^{\prime}-Ax-\tilde{b}\|^{2}\right)\\
\times\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2\cdot 3}\left(\|\varepsilon\|^{2}+2\bigg\langle\varepsilon,\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}}\left(A^{\prime}x+\tilde{b}^{\prime}+3(Ax+\tilde{b})-2(\theta+x)\right)\bigg\rangle\right)\right)\;,

(157)

where
C
does not depend on
A
nor
\tilde{b}
. Hence,

\mathbb{E}\left(\left(\frac{p_{\theta}(f(\varepsilon,\phi^{\prime};x)|x)}{q_{\phi}(f(\varepsilon,\phi^{\prime};x)|x)}\right)^{1-\alpha}\right)=C^{1-\alpha}\exp\left(\frac{3(1-\alpha)}{4}\|A^{\prime}x+\tilde{b}^{\prime}-Ax-\tilde{b}\|^{2}\right)\\
\times\mathbb{E}\left(\exp\left(-\frac{1-\alpha}{2\cdot 3}\left(\|\varepsilon\|^{2}+2\bigg\langle\varepsilon,\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}}\left(A^{\prime}x+\tilde{b}^{\prime}+3(Ax+\tilde{b})-2(\theta+x)\right)\bigg\rangle\right)\right)\right)\;,

with

\mathbb{E}\left(\exp\left(-\frac{1-\alpha}{2\cdot 3}\left(\|\varepsilon\|^{2}+2\bigg\langle\varepsilon,\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}}\left(A^{\prime}x+\tilde{b}^{\prime}+3(Ax+\tilde{b})-2(\theta+x)\right)\bigg\rangle\right)\right)\right)\\
=\left(\frac{3}{4-\alpha}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\exp\left(\frac{(1-\alpha)^{2}}{4(4-\alpha)}\bigg\|A^{\prime}x+\tilde{b}^{\prime}+3(Ax+\tilde{b})-2(\theta+x)\bigg\|^{2}\right).

Consequently: for all
\phi,\phi^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}
,

\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha})}=\exp\left(-\frac{1-\alpha}{2\cdot 3}\left(\|\varepsilon\|^{2}+2\bigg\langle\varepsilon,\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}}\left(A^{\prime}x+\tilde{b}^{\prime}+3(Ax+\tilde{b})-2(\theta+x)\right)\bigg\rangle\right)\right)\\
\times\left(\frac{4-\alpha}{3}\right)^{d/2}\exp\left(-\frac{(1-\alpha)^{2}}{4(4-\alpha)}\bigg\|A^{\prime}x+\tilde{b}^{\prime}+3(Ax+\tilde{b})-2(\theta+x)\bigg\|^{2}\right).

(158)

Hence, for all
k=1,\ldots,d
,

\partial_{\theta_{k}}\left(\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha})}\right)\\
=(1-\alpha)\left(\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}\varepsilon^{(k)}+\frac{1-\alpha}{4-\alpha}[4(\tilde{a}_{k}x_{k}+\tilde{b}_{k})-2(\theta_{k}+x_{k})]\right)\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha})}

(159)

and we also have

\displaystyle\partial_{\tilde{b}_{k}}\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha})}=-2\partial_{\theta_{k}}\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha})},

(160)

where
\varepsilon^{(k)}
denotes the k-th element of
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Furthermore,

\displaystyle\left.\left[\partial_{\tilde{b}^{\prime}_{k}}\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha})}\right]\right|_{\phi^{\prime}=\phi}=-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\theta_{k}}\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha})}.

(161)

In addition,

\theta_{k}\text{-}V^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)\\
=\mathbb{E}\left(\left(\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}\varepsilon^{(k)}+\frac{1-\alpha}{4-\alpha}[4(\tilde{a}_{k}x_{k}+\tilde{b}_{k})-2(\theta_{k}+x_{k})]\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha})}\right)^{2}\right)

(162)

and now using (
158
), we have that: for all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}
,

\tilde{q}(\varepsilon)\left(\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha})}\right)^{\ell}=\left(\frac{4-\alpha}{3}\right)^{d\ell/2}\exp\left(-\frac{(1-\alpha)^{2}\ell}{4(4-\alpha)}\|\mu\|^{2}\right)\\
(2\pi)^{-d/2}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|\varepsilon\|^{2}\right)\exp\left(-\frac{(1-\alpha)\ell}{2\cdot 3}\left(\|\varepsilon\|^{2}+2\bigg\langle\varepsilon,\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}}\mu\bigg\rangle\right)\right)

where for convenience we have denoted
\mu_{k}=4(\tilde{a}_{k}x_{k}+\tilde{b}_{k})-2(\theta_{k}+x_{k})
for all
k=1\ldots d
and
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{d})
. Thus,

\tilde{q}(\varepsilon)\left(\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha}}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{1-\alpha})}\right)^{\ell}\\
=h(\ell)\left(\frac{2\pi\cdot 3}{3+(1-\alpha)\ell}\right)^{-d/2}\exp\left(-\frac{3+(1-\alpha)\ell}{2\cdot 3}\bigg\|\varepsilon+\frac{(1-\alpha)\ell}{3+(1-\alpha)\ell}\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}}\mu\bigg\|^{2}\right)

(163)

with

\displaystyle h(\ell)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{4-\alpha}{3}\right)^{d\ell/2}\exp\left(-\frac{(1-\alpha)^{2}\ell}{4(4-\alpha)}\|\mu\|^{2}\right)\left(\frac{3}{3+(1-\alpha)\ell}\right)^{d/2}\exp\left(\frac{(1-\alpha)^{2}\ell^{2}}{4(3+(1-\alpha)\ell)}\|\mu\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{4-\alpha}{3}\right)^{d\ell/2}\exp\left(\frac{3(1-\alpha)^{2}\ell(\ell-1)}{4(4-\alpha)(3+(1-\alpha)\ell)}\|\mu\|^{2}\right)\left(\frac{3}{3+(1-\alpha)\ell}\right)^{d/2}.

Consequently, taking
\ell=2
in (
163
) and plugging this in (
162
),
we get that

\displaystyle\theta_{k}\text{-}V^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)=\frac{(4-\alpha)^{d}}{(15-6\alpha)^{d/2}}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{(1-\alpha)^{2}}{(4-\alpha)(5-2\alpha)}\frac{3}{2}\|\mu\|^{2}}\left(\frac{2}{5-2\alpha}+\frac{(1-\alpha)^{2}3^{2}}{(5-2\alpha)^{2}(4-\alpha)^{2}}\mu_{k}^{2}\right),

from which we can also deduce
\tilde{b}_{k}\text{-}V^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)
using (
160
). Similarly, using (
161
), we can deduce from the above
\tilde{b}_{k}\text{-}V^{(\alpha,\mathrm{DREP})}(\theta,\phi;x)
for all
\alpha\in(0,1)
. Now considering the case
\alpha=0
, we deduce using (
159
) and (
161
) with
\alpha=0
that:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\tilde{b}_{k}}\left(\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x))}\right)^{2}=-2\left(\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}\varepsilon^{(k)}+\frac{1}{4}\mu_{k}\right)\,\left(\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x))}\right)^{2}.

Hence,
A_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)
as defined in (
152
) with
\phi_{k}=\tilde{b}_{k}
becomes

\displaystyle A_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)=-\frac{1}{2}\left(\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}\varepsilon^{(k)}+\mu_{k}\right)\left(\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x))}\right)^{2}\;,

where we have used that
\left.\left[\partial_{\tilde{b}^{\prime}_{k}}\log q_{\phi^{\prime}}(f(\varepsilon,\phi;x)|x)\right]\right|_{\phi^{\prime}=\phi}=\sqrt{3/2}\penalty\ \varepsilon^{(k)}
. Now using (
163
) with
\alpha=0
and
\ell=2
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(A_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x))=-\frac{3}{2\times 5}\frac{4^{d}}{15^{d/2}}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{3}{8\times 5}\|\mu\|^{2}}\mu_{k}.

Next, using the above as well as (
163
) with
\alpha=0
and
\ell=2
and (
151
) with
\phi_{k}=\tilde{b}_{k}
, we can compute all the terms appearing in
\tilde{b}_{k}\text{-}V^{(0,\mathrm{DREP})}(\theta,\phi;x)
since

\displaystyle\tilde{b}_{k}\text{-}V^{(0,\mathrm{DREP})}(\theta,\phi;x)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}(A_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{2})-4\mathbb{E}\left(A_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x))}\right)\mathbb{E}\left(A_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(4\mathbb{E}\left(\left(\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x))}\right)^{2}\right)-1\right)\mathbb{E}(A_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x))^{2}

with

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(A_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)^{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}\left(\left(\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}\varepsilon^{(k)}+\mu_{k}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x))}\right)^{4}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{4}{3}\right)^{2d}\left(\frac{3}{7}\right)^{d/2}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{9}{4\cdot 7}\|\mu\|^{2}}\,\left(\frac{2}{7}+\frac{3^{2}}{7^{2}}\mu_{k}^{2}\right)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(A_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x))}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\left(\left(\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}\varepsilon^{(k)}+\mu_{k}\right)\left(\frac{\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)}{\mathbb{E}(\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\varepsilon;x))}\right)^{3}\right)=\frac{1}{4}h(3)\mu_{k}.

Thus, we finally get

\displaystyle\tilde{b}_{k}\text{-}V^{(0,\mathrm{DREP})}(\theta,\phi;x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{4}{3}\right)^{2d}\left(\frac{3}{7}\right)^{d/2}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{9}{4\cdot 7}\|\mu\|^{2}}\left(\frac{2}{7}+\frac{3^{2}}{7^{2}}\mu_{k}^{2}\right)

(164)

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\frac{4}{3}\right)^{3d/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{d/2}e^{\frac{3}{4\cdot 4}\|\mu\|^{2}}\frac{3}{2\cdot 5}\frac{4^{d}}{15^{d/2}}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{3}{8\times 5}\|\mu\|^{2}}\mu_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(4\frac{4^{d}}{15^{d/2}}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{3}{8\cdot 5}\|\mu\|^{2}}-1\right)\frac{3^{2}}{2^{2}\times 5^{2}}\frac{4^{2d}}{15^{d}}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{3}{4\times 5}\|\mu\|^{2}}\mu_{k}^{2}.

Lastly, we obtain the desired expressions in
Example

2
by taking
Ax+\tilde{b}=(\theta+x)/2+\epsilon\boldsymbol{u}_{d}
with
\epsilon>0
so that
\mu_{k}=4\epsilon
and
\|\mu\|^{2}=16d\epsilon^{2}
. Notice in particular that (
164
) becomes:

\tilde{b}_{k}\text{-}V^{(0,\mathrm{DREP})}(\theta,\phi;x)=\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{4}{3}\right)^{2d}\left(\frac{3}{7}\right)^{d/2}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{36}{7}d\epsilon^{2}}\,\left(\frac{2}{7}+\left(\frac{12\epsilon}{7}\right)^{2}\right)\\
-\left(\frac{4}{3}\right)^{3d/2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{d/2}e^{3d\epsilon^{2}}\frac{3}{2\cdot 5}\frac{4^{d}}{15^{d/2}}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{6}{5}d\epsilon^{2}}\mu_{k}^{2}+\left(4\frac{4^{d}}{15^{d/2}}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{6}{5}d\epsilon^{2}}-1\right)\frac{3^{2}}{2^{2}\times 5^{2}}\frac{4^{2d}}{15^{d}}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{12}{5}d\epsilon^{2}}\mu_{k}^{2}.

(165)

Appendix D
Deferred proofs and results for Section 3.2

D.1
L^{p}
norms

For a real valued random variable
X
we denote by
\|X\|_{p}
its
L^{p}

norm, that is,
\left\|X\right\|_{\infty}
denotes the smallest a.s. upper bound
of
|X|
and, for all
1\leq p<\infty
,

\left\|X\right\|_{p}=\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\left|X\right|^{p}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\;.

Recall that the
L^{p}
space of random variables with finite

L^{p}
-norms is a Banach space when endowed with this
L^{p}
-norm and
that, by Jensen’s inequality we have
\left\|X\right\|_{p}\leq\left\|X\right\|_{p^{\prime}}
for
all
p\leq p^{\prime}
.
We will also repeatedly use the Hölder inequality

\|XY\|_{1}\leq\|X\|_{p}\,\|Y\|_{p^{\prime}}\;,

which holds for all
X\in L^{p}
and

Y\in L^{p^{\prime}}
with
p,p^{\prime}\in[1,\infty]
such that
1/p+1/p^{\prime}=1
.

D.2
Preliminary results for the proofs of
Proposition

1

In the following we will use
the asymptotic equivalence

1-\Phi\left(u\right)=u^{-1}\varphi(u)(1+o(1))\quad\text{as $u\to\infty$,}

(166)

where
\varphi
denotes the standard Normal density and
\Phi
denotes the cumulative distribution function of the standard Normal distribution. In fact it is easy to show that the
o
-term in this asymptotic
equivalence is non-negative as soon as
u>0
, since, in this case,

1-\Phi\left(u\right)=\int_{u}^{\infty}\varphi(y)\;\mathrm{d}y\leq u^{-1}\int_{u}^{\infty}y\,\varphi(y)\;\mathrm{d}y=u^{-1}\varphi(u).

(167)

We then have the following two lemmas.

Lemma 5

Let
Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{N}
be i.i.d. normal random variables and set

M_{N}=\max_{1\leq i\leq n}Y_{i}
. Then for all

c>0
, we have, as
N\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(M_{N}>\sqrt{2\,(1+c)\,\log N}\right)=O\left(\left(\log N\right)^{-1/2}\,N^{-c}\right),

(168)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(M_{N}<\sqrt{2\,(1+c)^{-1}\,\log N}\right)=O\left(\mathrm{e}^{-N^{c/(1+c)}/\sqrt{4\pi\log N}}\right).

(169)

Proof.

For any
u\in\mathbb{R}
and
N\geq 1
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left(M_{N}>u\right)=1-\exp\left(N\log\left(1-\left(1-\Phi\left(u\right)\right)\right)\right)\;.

Then using (
166
) with
u=\sqrt{2x\log N}
and
x>0
, we have, as
N\to\infty
,

1-\Phi\left(u\right)=\frac{N^{-x}}{\sqrt{4\pi\,x\log N}}\,(1+o(1)).

We successively get the following assertions depending on where
x

lies w.r.t. 1

\displaystyle\text{if $x>1$, as $N\to\infty$,}\quad\mathbb{P}\left(M_{N}>\sqrt{2x\log N}\right)=\frac{N^{1-x}}{\sqrt{4\pi\,x\,\log N}}\;\left(1+o(1)\right),

\displaystyle\text{if $x\in(0,1)$, as $N\to\infty$,}\quad\mathbb{P}\left(M_{N}\leq\sqrt{2x\log N}\right)=O\left(\exp\left(-N^{1-x}/\sqrt{4\pi\log N}\right)\right),

The two bounds (
168
)
and (
169
) easily follow.
∎

The next lemma extends

Daudel et al., 2023
, Lemma 1
, which follows from the case
m=1
.

Lemma 6

Let
Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{N}
be i.i.d. normal random variables and set

M_{N}=\max_{1\leq i\leq n}Y_{i}
. Let
m\in[1,\infty)
. Then, as
N\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\left|M_{N}\right|^{m}\right)=\left(2\log N\right)^{m/2}\left(1+O\left(\frac{\log\log N}{\log N}\right)\right)

(170)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\left(M_{N}\right)_{-}^{m}\right)=O\left(\rho^{N}\right)\quad\text{for any $\rho\in(1/2,1)$},

(171)

where
(x)_{-}=\max(-x,0)
denotes the negative part of
x
.

Proof.

The Gaussian distribution belongs to the
maximum domain of attraction of the Gumbel distribution. More
precisely, we have
(see
Haan & Ferreira, 2007
)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(a_{N}^{-1}\left(M_{N}-b_{N}\right)\leq x\right)=\exp(-\mathrm{e}^{-x}),

with
a_{N}=1/\sqrt{2\log N}
,
b_{N}=\sqrt{2\log N}-\frac{1}{2}(\log\log N+\log 4\pi)/(\sqrt{2\log N})
. Since the
Gaussian distribution has finite moments of all orders and so does
the Gumbel distribution,
Pickands, 1968
, Theorem
2.1
yields

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left(\left|L_{N}\right|^{m}\right)=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\left|x\right|^{m}\;\exp(-x-\mathrm{e}^{-x})\;\mathrm{d}x,

where we have set
L_{N}=a_{N}^{-1}(M_{N}-b_{N})
. Now writing
b_{N}^{-1}M_{N}=1+\frac{a_{N}}{b_{N}}L_{N}
and since
m\in[1,\infty)
by assumption, the Minkowski
inequality yields

\left|\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\left|b_{N}^{-1}M_{N}\right|^{m}\right)\right)^{1/m}-1\right|\leq\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\left|b_{N}^{-1}M_{N}-1\right|^{m}\right)\right)^{1/m}=\frac{a_{N}}{b_{N}}\;\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\left|L_{N}\right|^{m}\right)\right)^{1/m}.

Hence
\mathbb{E}\left(\left|M_{N}\right|^{m}\right)=b_{N}^{m}\left(1+O\left(\frac{a_{N}}{b_{N}}\right)\right)^{m}
, which
leads to (
170
). To
get (
171
), we use that

\mathbb{E}\left(\left(M_{N}\right)_{-}^{m}\right)=\mathbb{E}\left(\left|M_{N}\right|^{m}\mathbbm{1}_{\left\{M_{N}<0\right\}}\right)
,
and, for any
p>1
, the Hölder inequality leads to

\mathbb{E}\left(\left(M_{N}\right)_{-}^{m}\right)\leq\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\left|M_{N}\right|^{m\,p}\right)\right)^{1/p}\,\left(\mathbb{P}\left(M_{N}<0\right)\right)^{(p-1)/p}=\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\left|M_{N}\right|^{m\,p}\right)\right)^{1/p}\,\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{N\,(p-1)/p}\;.

Since (
170
) also holds with
m
replaced
by
m\,p
, we get the claimed bound by choosing
p
large enough to have
\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{(p-1)/p}\in(\frac{1}{2},\rho)
with
\rho\in(\frac{1}{2},1)
.
∎

We next present a key proposition, which provides a general bound that will be used to show the collapse of self-normalized weighted averages (in the sense that one weight dominates over all others in the self-normalized weighted averages).

Proposition 7

Let

\left(\left(W_{i},\tilde{Y}_{i}\right)\right)_{i\geq 1}
be a sequence of i.i.d.

\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}\times\mathbb{R}
-valued random variables. Let
F
denote the distribution function of
\log W_{1}
, and its generalized inverse by

F^{\leftarrow}
. In addition, let
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
and
let us define, for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
,

\zeta(s)=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\mathrm{e}^{F^{\leftarrow}\circ\Phi(Z)-F^{\leftarrow}\circ\Phi(s)}\,\right|\,Z\leq s\right]}{1-\Phi\left(s\right)}.

(172)

Let us further define, for all
N\geq 1
,
\tilde{Y}_{N}^{*}=\max\left(\left|\tilde{Y}_{1}\right|,\dots,\left|\tilde{Y}_{N}\right|\right)
and for
all
u\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
,
m\in[1,\infty)
and
p\in(1,\infty)
,

\tilde{\zeta}_{N}^{(m,p)}(u,s)=N\,\left(\left\|\tilde{Y}_{1}\right\|_{p\,m}^{1/m}\;s^{(p-1)/p}+\left\|\tilde{Y}_{1}\,\mathbbm{1}_{\left\{\tilde{Y}_{N}^{*}>u\right\}}\right\|_{m}\right)\;.

(173)

Then, there exists a non-decreasing random sequence

\left(J_{N}\right)_{N\geq 1}
such that, for all
N\geq 1
,
J_{N}
is valued in

\left\{1,\dots,N\right\}
with
W_{J_{N}}=\max\left(W_{1},\dots,W_{N}\right)

and, for all
m,\delta\in[1,\infty)
,

\underline{b}<2<\overline{b}
,
p\in(1,\infty)
,
u>0
and

N\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\overline{W}_{i,N}^{\,\delta}\,\tilde{Y}_{i}-\tilde{Y}_{J_{N}}\right\|_{m}

\displaystyle\leq C\,\left(u\,\sup_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle b\in\left[\underline{b},\overline{b}\right]}}{{q=1,m}}}\left(\zeta(b\,\sqrt{\log N})\right)^{\frac{q}{m}}+\tilde{\zeta}_{N}^{(m,p)}\left(u,\left(\log N\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\,N^{1-\frac{\overline{b}}{2}}\right)\right)\;,

(174)

where
C>0
is a constant only depending on
p
,
\underline{b}
,

\overline{b}
,
\delta
and
m
, and

\overline{W}_{i,N}
denotes the self-normalized weight defined by

\overline{W}_{i,N}:=\left(\sum_{1\leq k\leq N}W_{k}\right)^{-1}\,W_{i}\;,\qquad 1\leq i\leq N.

Proof.

Let us denote by
Q
the probability kernel of a regular version of
the conditional distribution of
\tilde{Y}_{1}
given
W_{1}
(which exists since the real line is a Polish space, see

Douc et al., 2018
, Thorem B.3.11
), that is

\mathbb{P}\left[\left.\tilde{Y}_{1}\leq\tilde{y}\,\right|\,W_{1}\right]=Q(W_{1},(-\infty,\tilde{y}])

\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for all

\tilde{y}\in\mathbb{R}
. Further denote the associated conditional
generalized inverse by
Q^{\leftarrow}
, that is

\mbox{$\forall w\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}$, $\forall u\in(0,1)$},\quad Q^{\leftarrow}(w,u)=\inf\left\{\tilde{y}\in\mathbb{R}\;:\;Q(w,(-\infty,\tilde{y}])\geq u\right\}.

Recall that
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
and let
U
be independent of

Z
with uniform distribution on
[0,1]
. By definition of
F

and
Q
,
\left(W_{1},\tilde{Y}_{1}\right)
have the same distribution as

\left(\mathrm{e}^{F^{\leftarrow}\circ\Phi(Z)},Q^{\leftarrow}(\mathrm{e}^{F^{\leftarrow}\circ\Phi(Z)},U)\right)
.
Therefore, from now on, we
let
\left(Z_{i}\right)_{i\geq 1}
and
\left(U_{i}\right)_{i\geq 1}
be two independent
i.i.d. sequence distributed as
Z
and
U
, respectively and set,
without loss of generality,

\text{$\forall i\geq 1$},\quad W_{i}=\mathrm{e}^{F^{\leftarrow}\circ\Phi(Z_{i})}\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{Y}_{i}=Q^{\leftarrow}(W_{i},U_{i})\;.

(175)

Now denoting by
I_{N}
the (random) index in
\left\{1,\dots,N\right\}

such that
Z_{I_{N}}=\max(Z_{1},\dots,Z_{N})
, we have that
I_{N}
is
a.s. uniquely defined since
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{N}
are independent and
\Phi
is continuous. Furthermore, as required in the proposition,
\left(I_{N}\right)_{N\geq 1}
is a non-decreasing random sequence
such that: for all
N\geq 1
,
I_{N}
is valued in
\left\{1,\dots,N\right\}
with
W_{I_{N}}=\max\left(W_{1},\dots,W_{N}\right)
.

Next let
m,\delta\in[1,\infty)
,
\underline{b}<1<\overline{b}
and
p\in(1,\infty)
. It remains to show that there exists a
constant
C>0
only depending on these constants such that the bound (
174
) holds for all
u>0
and
N\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
. To this end, we first write

\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\overline{W}_{i,N}\right)^{\delta}\,\tilde{Y}_{i}=\tilde{Y}_{I_{N}}-A_{N}+B_{N}\;,

where, denoting
J_{N}:=\left\{1,\dots,N\right\}\setminus\left\{I_{N}\right\}
, we set

\displaystyle A_{N}:=\left(1-\left(\overline{W}_{I_{N},N}\right)^{\delta}\right)\tilde{Y}_{I_{N}}\quad\text{and}\quad B_{N}:=\sum_{i\in J_{N}}\left(\overline{W}_{i,N}\right)^{\delta}\,\tilde{Y}_{i}\;.

To evaluate the left-hand side
of (
174
), that is
\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\overline{W}_{i,N}^{\,\delta}\,\tilde{Y}_{i}-\tilde{Y}_{I_{N}}\right\|_{m}
, the idea is to separate the integration domain in
\left\{\underline{b}\,\leq\frac{Z_{I_{N}}}{\sqrt{\log N}}\leq\overline{b}\right\}\cap\left\{\tilde{Y}_{N}^{*}\leq u\right\}
and its complementary set. More specifically, to prove (
174
) it
suffices to show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\left|A_{N}\right|^{m}\,\mathbbm{1}_{\left\{\underline{b}\leq\frac{Z_{I_{N}}}{\sqrt{\log N}}\leq\overline{b}\right\}\cap\left\{\tilde{Y}_{N}^{*}\leq u\right\}}\right)\leq\delta\,C_{2}\;u^{m}\;\sup_{{b\in\left[\underline{b},\overline{b}\right]},{q=1,m}}\left(\zeta(b\,\sqrt{\log N})\right)^{q}

(176)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\left|B_{N}\right|^{m}\,\mathbbm{1}_{\left\{\underline{b}\leq\frac{Z_{I_{N}}}{\sqrt{\log N}}\leq\overline{b}\right\}\cap\left\{\tilde{Y}_{N}^{*}\leq u\right\}}\right)\leq C_{2}\;u^{m}\;\sup_{{b\in\left[\underline{b},\overline{b}\right]},{q=1,m}}\left(\zeta(b\,\sqrt{\log N})\right)^{q}

(177)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left|\tilde{Y}_{i}\right|\right)^{m}\,\mathbbm{1}_{\left\{\frac{Z_{I_{N}}}{\sqrt{\log N}}\notin\left[\underline{b},\overline{b}\right]\right\}\cup\left\{\tilde{Y}_{N}^{*}>u\right\}}\right)\leq C_{1}\;\left(\tilde{\zeta}_{N}^{(m,p)}(u,\left(\log N\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\,N^{1-\overline{b}/2})\right)^{m},

(178)

where
C_{1}
only depends on
p
,
\underline{b}
and
\overline{b}

and
C_{2}
only depends on
m
. The desired result will then follow by combining (
176
), (
177
), the Minkowski inequality, (
178
) and the fact that since
\left|A_{N}\right|\leq\left|\tilde{Y}_{I_{N}}\right|
and

\left|B_{N}\right|\leq\sum_{i\in J_{N}}\left|\tilde{Y}_{i}\right|
, we have
\left|A_{N}\right|+\left|B_{N}\right|\leq\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left|\tilde{Y}_{i}\right|
.
Let us thus prove separately that (i) the inequalities (
176
)
and (
177
) hold and (ii) the inequality (
178
) holds.

(i)

Proof
of (
176
) and (
177
).
For all
N\geq 2
, set

\displaystyle D_{N}=\sum_{i\in J_{N}}\frac{W_{i}}{W_{I_{N}}}.

(179)

Then, for all
N\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\left|A_{N}\right|

\displaystyle=\left(1-(1+D_{N})^{-\delta}\right)\,\left|\tilde{Y}_{I_{N}}\right|\leq\left(\left(1+D_{N}\right)^{\delta}-1\right)\,\left|\tilde{Y}_{I_{N}}\right|\leq\delta\,D_{N}\,\tilde{Y}_{N}^{*},

where in the last inequality we used the definition of
\tilde{Y}_{N}^{*}
and the fact that
(1+x)^{\delta}\leq 1+\delta x
for all

\delta\geq 1
and
x\geq 0
. Furthermore, for all
N\geq 2
,

\left|B_{N}\right|\leq\left(1+D_{N}\right)^{-\delta}\,{\sum_{i\in J_{N}}\left(\frac{W_{i}}{W_{I_{N}}}\right)^{\delta}\,\left|\tilde{Y}_{i}\right|}\leq\sum_{i\in J_{N}}\left(\frac{W_{i}}{W_{I_{N}}}\right)^{\delta}\,\left|\tilde{Y}_{i}\right|\leq D_{N}\,\tilde{Y}_{N}^{*}\;,

where we used that
\delta\geq 1
, and
W_{i}\leq W_{I_{N}}
for all

i\in J_{N}
. To get (
176
)
and (
177
), notice that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(D_{N}^{m}\,(\tilde{Y}_{N}^{*})^{m}\penalty\ \mathbbm{1}_{\left\{\underline{b}\leq\frac{Z_{I_{N}}}{\sqrt{\log N}}\leq\overline{b}\right\}\cap\left\{\tilde{Y}_{N}^{*}\leq u\right\}}\right)\leq u^{m}\mathbb{E}\left(D_{N}^{m}\,\mathbbm{1}_{\left\{\underline{b}\leq\frac{Z_{I_{N}}}{\sqrt{\log N}}\leq\overline{b}\right\}}\right),

thus it only remains to show
that there exists
C_{2}>0
which only depends on
m
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(D_{N}^{m}\,\mathbbm{1}_{\left\{\underline{b}\leq\frac{Z_{I_{N}}}{\sqrt{\log N}}\leq\overline{b}\right\}}\right)\leq C_{2}\;\sup_{{b\in\left[\underline{b},\overline{b}\right]},{q=1,m}}\left(\zeta(b\,\sqrt{\log N})\right)^{q}.

(180)

By definition of
D_{N}
in (
179
) and using (
175
), we have that

D_{N}=\sum_{k\in J_{N}}\mathrm{e}^{F^{\leftarrow}\circ\Phi(Z_{i})-F^{\leftarrow}\circ\Phi(Z_{I_{N}})}

hence conditioning on
Z_{I_{N}}
, we get

\mathbb{E}\left(D_{N}^{m}\,\mathbbm{1}_{\left\{\underline{b}\leq\frac{Z_{I_{N}}}{\sqrt{\log N}}\leq\overline{b}\right\}}\right)\\
=\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\left\{\underline{b}\leq\frac{Z_{I_{N}}}{\sqrt{\log N}}\leq\overline{b}\right\}}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\mathrm{e}^{F^{\leftarrow}\circ\Phi(Z_{i})-F^{\leftarrow}\circ\Phi(s)}\right)^{m}\,\right|\,Z_{i}\leq Z_{I_{N}}\,,\,1\leq i\leq N-1\right]\right).

(181)

Since the
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{N}
are i.i.d. with the same distribution as
Z
, by the Rosenthal inequality
(see Therorem 2.12
Petrov, 1995
)
, we
have, for a constant
C_{4}>0
only depending on
m
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\mathrm{e}^{F^{\leftarrow}\circ\Phi(Z_{i})-F^{\leftarrow}\circ\Phi(s)}\right)^{m}\,\right|\,Z_{i}\leq Z_{I_{N}}\,,\,1\leq i\leq N-1\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq C_{4}\;\left[(N-1)\,\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\mathrm{e}^{m\,(F^{\leftarrow}\circ\Phi(Z)-F^{\leftarrow}\circ\Phi(s))}\,\right|\,Z\leq Z_{I_{N}}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\left.+\left((N-1)\,\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\mathrm{e}^{F^{\leftarrow}\circ\Phi(Z)-F^{\leftarrow}\circ\Phi(s)}\,\right|\,Z\leq Z_{I_{N}}\right]\right)^{m}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq C_{4}\;\sum_{q=1,m}\left((N-1)\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\mathrm{e}^{F^{\leftarrow}\circ\Phi(Z)-F^{\leftarrow}\circ\Phi(s)}\,\right|\,Z\leq Z_{I_{N}}\right]\right)^{q},

where, in the last inequality, we used that
m\geq 1
and that the
exponent of the exponential in the first line is non-positive when
conditioning on
\left\{Z\leq Z_{I_{N}}\right\}
.
The expectation appearing in the last line is equal to

\left(1-\Phi\left(Z_{I_{N}}\right)\right)\,\zeta(Z_{I_{N}})
.
Using this with (
181
), we get
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(D_{N}^{m}\,\mathbbm{1}_{\left\{\underline{b}\frac{Z_{I_{N}}}{\sqrt{\log N}}\leq\overline{b}\right\}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{4}\;\sum_{q=1,m}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\left\{\underline{b}\leq\frac{Z_{I_{N}}}{\sqrt{\log N}}\leq\overline{b}\right\}}\,\left\{(N-1)(1-\Phi(Z_{I_{N}}))\,\zeta(Z_{I_{N}})\right\}^{q}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\,C_{4}\max_{q=1,m}\left((N-1)^{q}\,\mathbb{E}\left(\left(1-\Phi(Z_{I_{N}})\right)^{q}\right)\;\sup_{b\in\left[\underline{b},\overline{b}\right]}\left(\zeta(b\,\sqrt{\log N})\right)^{q}\right)\;.

Since
\Phi
is continuous, we further have that, for any
q\geq 1
,

\mathbb{E}\left(\left(1-\Phi(Z_{I_{N}})\right)^{q}\right)=\mathbb{E}\left((1-U_{(N,N)})^{q}\right)=N\int_{0}^{1}(1-x)^{q}\,x^{N-1}\,\mathrm{d}x=\frac{\Gamma(q+1)\,N!}{\Gamma(N+q+1)}\leq\frac{\Gamma(q+1)}{(N+1)^{q}}\;,

where
U_{(N,N)}
is the maximum of
N
i.i.d. random variables with
uniform distribution in
[0,1]
. Hence (
180
) holds with

C_{2}=2\,C_{4}\,\Gamma(m+1)
, which concludes the proof of (
176
) and (
177
).

(ii)

Proof
of (
178
).

Since
m\geq 1
,
x\mapsto x^{m}
is convex and we have

\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left|\tilde{Y}_{i}\right|\right)^{m}\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left|\tilde{Y}_{i}\right|^{m}
, leading to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left|\tilde{Y}_{i}\right|\right)^{m}\,\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbf{D}_{N}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq N^{m-1}\;\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\left(\left|\tilde{Y}_{i}\right|^{m}\,\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbf{D}_{N}}\right)

\displaystyle=N^{m}\,\mathbb{E}\left(\left|\tilde{Y}_{1}\right|^{m}\,\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbf{D}_{N}}\right)\;,

where we used that the domain
\mathbf{D}_{N}=\left\{\frac{Z_{I_{N}}}{\sqrt{\log N}}\notin\left[\underline{b},\overline{b}\right]\right\}\cup\left\{\tilde{Y}_{N}^{*}>u\right\}
is stable by permutation of the indices
i

within
\left\{1,\dots,N\right\}
so that

\left|\tilde{Y}_{i}\right|^{m}\,\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbf{D}_{N}}
has the same
distribution as
\left|\tilde{Y}_{1}\right|^{m}\,\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbf{D}_{N}}
for
all
i=1,\dots,N
. Now we note that
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbf{D}_{N}}\leq\mathbbm{1}_{\left\{\frac{Z_{I_{N}}}{\sqrt{\log N}}\notin\left[\underline{b},\overline{b}\right]\right\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\left\{\tilde{Y}_{N}^{*}>u\right\}}
and, by
definition of
\tilde{\zeta}_{N}^{(m,p)}
in (
173
), to
get (
178
) with
C_{1}=\max\left(1,C_{3}^{(p-1)/p}\right)
, it only remains to show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\left|\tilde{Y}_{i}\right|^{m}\,\mathbbm{1}_{\left\{\frac{Z_{I_{N}}}{\sqrt{\log N}}\notin\left[\underline{b},\overline{b}\right]\right\}}\right)\leq\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\left|\tilde{Y}_{1}\right|^{p\,m}\right)\right)^{1/p}\,\left(C_{3}\,\left(\log N\right)^{-1/2}\,N^{1-\overline{b}/2}\right)^{(p-1)/p}\;,

(182)

for a certain
C_{3}>0
only depending on
\underline{b}
and
\overline{b}
.
Using
Lemma

5
with
c=\overline{b}/2-1
and

c=2/\underline{b}-1
successively in (
168
)
and (
169
), we indeed find for such a
constant
C_{3}
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{Z_{I_{N}}}{\sqrt{\log N}}\notin\left[\underline{b},\overline{b}\right]\right)\leq C_{3}\,\left(\left(\log N\right)^{-1/2}\,N^{1-\overline{b}/2}\right)\;.

Then we get (
182
) by applying
the Hölder inequality and the proof
of (
178
) is concluded.

∎

Remark 7

Proposition

7
investigates the behavior of the weighted average of
\left(\tilde{Y}_{i}\right)_{i=1,\dots,N}
when using self-normalized positive weights
\left(\overline{W}_{i,N}\right)_{i=1,\dots,N}
to some power
\delta\geq 1
. More
precisely, (
174
) provides a bound
of the
L^{m}
-norm of the error when approximating this average by a
single
\tilde{Y}_{I_{N}}
, with
I_{N}
corresponding to an index
with maximal weight.

Under standard moment conditions, and using the law of large numbers, such an average should be well approximated as
N\to\infty
by
N^{1-\delta}\mathbb{E}\left(W_{1}^{\delta}\tilde{Y}_{1}\right)\left(\mathbb{E}\left(W_{1}\right)\right)^{-\delta}

rather than by the (random)
\tilde{Y}_{I_{N}}
as
in (
174
).
However, we will apply (
174
) with

F
depending on
N
in such a way that the maximal weight
W_{I_{N}}
tends to dominate over all other weights.

In particular, in our applications
of
Proposition

7
, we will take
advantage of the fact that the constant
C
does not depend on
F

in the
bound (
174
). Furthermore,
since (
174
) holds for any

u>0
, we will choose
u
for a given
N
so that the right-hand
side of (
174
) is as small as
possible. This will be achieved by compromising between the
u
in
the first term (which increases as
u
increases) and the second
expectation in (
173
) defining

\tilde{\zeta}_{N}^{(m,p)}(u,s)
which decreases as
u

increases. Finally, the first term between the parentheses
of (
173
) will be made small by taking

\overline{b}
large enough to compensate the
N
in front of the
parentheses.

D.3
Additional notation, useful first properties and derivations

In the remaining of
Appendix

D
, we let

\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}
and
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\ldots
be
i.i.d. copies of
\varepsilon
and we assume
that
(B3)
holds and so does

(B

df

REP

)
. We now introduce the following helpful notation:

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\theta,\phi}(\cdot;x)=\log\tilde{w}_{\theta,\phi,\phi}(\cdot;x)

(183)

\displaystyle B(\theta,\phi;x)^{2}=\mathbb{V}_{\varepsilon\sim\tilde{q}}\left(\Lambda_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)\right)=2\mathrm{KL}\left(\theta,\phi;x\right)

(184)

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{W}}^{(\alpha)}_{j,N}=\left(\sum_{k=1}^{N}\mathrm{e}^{(1-\alpha)\,\Lambda_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon_{k};x)}\right)^{-1}\,\mathrm{e}^{(1-\alpha)\,\Lambda_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon_{j};x)}\;,\qquad 1\leq j\leq N

(185)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\theta,\phi}(\cdot;x)=\frac{\Lambda_{\theta,\phi}(\cdot;x)-\mathbb{E}\left(\Lambda_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)\right)}{B(\theta,\phi;x)}

(186)

\displaystyle\widetilde{B}^{(\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)=\sqrt{\mathbb{V}\left(\partial_{\psi}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)\right)}\;.

(187)

Under
(B3)
, the expectation and variance
of
\Lambda_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)
are well
defined. Furthermore,
\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\theta,\phi}
is well defined in (
186
) if
B(\theta,\phi;x)>0
and
\widetilde{B}^{(\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)^{2}
is well defined if

(\theta^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})\mapsto\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\theta^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)

is well defined and differentiable in a neighborhood of

(\theta,\phi)
. Using
Proposition

4
, all
these conditions hold providing that
B(\theta,\phi;x)>0
(or
equivalently
\mathrm{KL}\left(\theta,\phi;x\right)>0
), which we assume in the following since we are interested in the setting where
N,\mathrm{KL}\left(\theta,\phi;x\right)\to\infty
. More precisely, we get the
following assertions:

(i)

We have that
\partial_{\psi}B(\theta,\phi;x)
is well defined;

(ii)

Since
B(\theta,\phi;x)>0
,

(\theta^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})\mapsto\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\theta^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}}(\varepsilon;x)

is well defined and differentiable in a neighborhood of
(\theta,\phi)
;

(iii)

We have that
\widetilde{B}^{(\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)
is well defined;

(iv)

We have that
\left(\left(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x),\partial_{\psi}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)\right)\right)_{(\theta,\phi)\in\Theta\times\Phi}

is a Gaussian process;

(v)

We can interchange the derivative
\partial_{\psi}
with the expectation
sign for
\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)
as well as its square.

Using Assertion
(v)
and since
\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)
is centered with variance 1, we then get that

\mathbb{E}\left(\partial_{\psi}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)\right)=\mathbb{C}\mathrm{ov}\left(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x),\partial_{\psi}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)\right)=0\;.

Combining this with Assertion
(iv)
, we thus have the following useful property:

(
\mathrm{P}^{\mathrm{REP}}
)

\left(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)\right)_{(\theta,\phi)\in\Theta\times\Phi}

and
\left(\partial_{\psi}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)\right)_{(\theta,\phi)\in\Theta\times\Phi}

are two independent and centered Gaussian processes.

Plugging in the notation we introduced in (
14
) and using
straightforward algebra, the REP gradient estimator reads

\displaystyle\psi\text{-}g_{1,N}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)

\displaystyle=\partial_{\psi}\mathbb{E}(\Lambda_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x))+\partial_{\psi}B(\theta,\phi;x)\;\sum_{j=1}^{N}\overline{\mathcal{W}}^{(\alpha)}_{j,N}\;\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon_{j};x)

\displaystyle\quad+B(\theta,\phi;x)\sum_{j=1}^{N}\overline{\mathcal{W}}^{(\alpha)}_{j,N}\;\partial_{\psi}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon_{j};x)\;.

(188)

Furthermore, differentiating
B^{2}(\theta,\phi;x)=\mathbb{V}\left(\Lambda_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)\right)
, we get that

\displaystyle B(\theta,\phi;x)\,\partial_{\psi}B(\theta,\phi;x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\psi}B^{2}(\theta,\phi;x)=\mathbb{C}\mathrm{ov}\left(\Lambda_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x),\partial_{\psi}\Lambda_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)\right)\;.

Next differentiating the product

B(\theta,\phi;x)\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)
, which equals
\Lambda_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)

up to a added non-random constant, we obtain the sum of two uncorrelated
variables and taking the variance leads to

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}\left(\partial_{\psi}\Lambda_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)\right)=\left(\partial_{\psi}B(\theta,\phi;x)\right)^{2}+\left(B(\theta,\phi;x)\,\widetilde{B}^{(\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)\right)^{2}\;.

(189)

Since we can rewrite the correlation from (
40
) as

\displaystyle\mathrm{C}_{\psi}(\theta,\phi;x)=\frac{\mathbb{C}\mathrm{ov}\left(\Lambda_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x),\partial_{\psi}\Lambda_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)\right)}{\sqrt{B(\theta,\phi;x)^{2}\mathbb{V}\left(\partial_{\psi}\Lambda_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x)\right)}}

we also get using the expressions above that

\displaystyle\mathrm{C}_{\psi}(\theta,\phi;x)=\frac{\partial_{\psi}B(\theta,\phi;x)}{\sqrt{\left(\partial_{\psi}B(\theta,\phi;x)\right)^{2}+\left(B(\theta,\phi;x)\,\widetilde{B}^{(\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)\right)^{2}}}\;.

(190)

To conclude this section we provide expression for
\mathrm{SNR}[\psi\text{-}g_{1,1}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)]
as well as
\mathrm{SNR}[\psi\text{-}g_{1,1}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)-\partial_{\psi}\ell(\theta;x)]
, where the latter is linked to
\mathrm{C}_{\psi}(\theta,\phi;x)
. Using (
188
) with
N=1
, we obtain that

\psi\text{-}g_{1,1}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)=\partial_{\psi}\mathbb{E}(\Lambda_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x))\\
+\partial_{\psi}B(\theta,\phi;x)\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon_{1};x)+B(\theta,\phi;x)\,\partial_{\psi}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon_{1};x)\;.

(191)

It follows from (
191
) and Property
(

P
REP

)
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}(\psi\text{-}g_{1,1}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x))=\left(\partial_{\psi}B(\theta,\phi;x)\right)^{2}+\left(B(\theta,\phi;x)\widetilde{B}^{(\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)\right)^{2}

(192)

\displaystyle\mathrm{SNR}[\psi\text{-}g_{1,1}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)]=\frac{\left|\partial_{\psi}\mathbb{E}(\Lambda_{\theta,\phi}(\varepsilon;x))\right|}{\sqrt{\left(\partial_{\psi}B(\theta,\phi;x)\right)^{2}+\left(B(\theta,\phi;x)\widetilde{B}^{(\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)\right)^{2}}}

(193)

and, using (
37
)
and (
190
), which leads to

\displaystyle\mathrm{SNR}[\psi\text{-}g_{1,1}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)-\partial_{\psi}\ell(\theta;x)]=B(\theta,\phi;x)\,\left|\mathrm{C}_{\psi}(\theta,\phi;x)\right|\;.

(194)

Notice in particular that we also have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{\psi}\ell(\theta;x)-\mathbb{E}(\psi\text{-}g_{1,1}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x))}{\mathbb{V}(\psi\text{-}g_{1,1}^{(\alpha,\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x))}

\displaystyle=\frac{{B(\theta,\phi;x)\partial_{\psi}B(\theta,\phi;x)}}{\sqrt{\left(\partial_{\psi}B(\theta,\phi;x)\right)^{2}+\left(B(\theta,\phi;x)\widetilde{B}^{(\mathrm{REP})}(\theta,\phi;x)\right)^{2}}}

\displaystyle=B(\theta,\phi;x)\,{\mathrm{C}_{\psi}(\theta,\phi;x)}

(195)

D.4
Proof of
Proposition

1
and
Theorem

5

We start with two lemmas.

Lemma 7

Let
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. Then, for all

s\in\mathbb{R}
and
\sigma>0
,

\mathbb{E}\left(\mathrm{e}^{\sigma(Z-s)}\,|\,Z\leq s\right)=\frac{\Phi(s-\sigma)}{\Phi(s)}\;\frac{\varphi(s)}{\varphi(\sigma-s)}\;.

(196)

It follows that, defining
\zeta
by (
172
) with

F^{\leftarrow}=\mu+\sigma\Phi^{-1}
where
\mu\in\mathbb{R}
and
\sigma>0
, there exists a universal
constant
C>0
such that, for all
\sigma\geq 2s\geq 1
,

\zeta(s)\leq C\;\frac{s}{\sigma}\;.

(197)

Proof.

We have, for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
\sigma>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\mathrm{e}^{\sigma(Z-s)}\,|\,Z\leq s\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Phi(s)}\,\mathbb{E}\left(\mathrm{e}^{\sigma(Z-s)}\,\mathbbm{1}_{\left\{Z\leq s\right\}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Phi(s)}\;\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\varphi(s)\,\varphi(Z-\sigma)}{\varphi(\sigma-s)\,\varphi(Z)}\mathbbm{1}_{\left\{Z\leq s\right\}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Phi(s)}\,\frac{\varphi(s)}{\varphi(\sigma-s)}\;\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\left\{Z+\sigma\leq s\right\}}\right),

where, in the last line, we used that
Z+\sigma
has density
u\mapsto\varphi(u-\sigma)
hence the likelihood ratio
\varphi(Z-\sigma)/\varphi(Z)
amounts to change
Z
into
Z+\sigma
. We thus get (
196
). Now, defining
\zeta
by (
172
) with

F^{\leftarrow}=\mu+\sigma\Phi^{-1}
, we get, for all
s>0
and
\sigma>0
,

\zeta(s)=\frac{1}{\Phi(s)}\frac{m(\sigma-s)}{m(s)},

where the function
m
corresponds to the Mills ratio
m(u)=\frac{1-\Phi(u)}{\varphi(u)}
with
u>0
. Since

\displaystyle\forall u>0,\quad\frac{u}{u^{2}+1}<m(u)<\frac{1}{u},

(see
Gordon, 1941
)
we finally deduce that: for all
\sigma>2s\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\zeta(s)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\Phi(1)}\frac{s^{2}+1}{s}\frac{1}{\sigma-s}\leq C\penalty\ \frac{s}{\sigma},

where
C={6}{\Phi(1)}^{-1}
and where we have used that
s\geq 1
and
s\leq{\sigma}/{2}
.
∎

Lemma 8

Let

\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\dots
be i.i.d. standard normal random variables
and set, for all
N=1,2,\dots
, and
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
,

\overline{W}_{j,N}(\beta):=\left(\sum_{k=1}^{N}\mathrm{e}^{\beta\,\xi_{k}}\right)^{-1}\,\mathrm{e}^{\beta\,\xi_{j}}\;,\qquad 1\leq j\leq N\;.

(198)

Let
\delta\geq 1
,
\lambda\in[0,1]
and
q\geq 1
. Then we have, as
N,\beta\to\infty
with
\sqrt{\log N}=o(\beta)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{N}(\overline{W}_{j,N}(\beta))^{\delta}\;\xi_{j}\right)=\left(2\,\log N\right)^{1/2}\left(1+o(1)\right),

(199)

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{N}[\lambda\overline{W}_{j,N}(\beta)+(1-\lambda)(\overline{W}_{j,N}(\beta))^{2}]\,\xi_{j}\right)=o\left(\log N\right),

(200)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left(\overline{W}_{j,N}(\beta)\right)^{\delta}\right)=1+o(1).

(201)

Proof.

We will first show that, for any
m\in[1,\infty)
, as
N,\beta\to\infty
with
\sqrt{\log N}=o(\beta)
,

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}(\overline{W}_{i,N}(\beta))^{\,\delta}\,\xi_{i}-\max(\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{N})\right\|_{m}=o\left(\sqrt{\log N}\right)\;,

(202)

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}(\overline{W}_{i,N}(\beta))^{\,\delta}-1\right\|_{m}=O\left(\left(\frac{\sqrt{\log N}}{\beta}\right)^{\frac{1}{m}}+N^{-\frac{q}{m}}\right)\;.

(203)

Then we will show that (
202
) implies
(
199
)-(
200
), and
that (
203
) implies
(
201
).

(i)

Proof of (
202
)
and (
203
)
. We use

Proposition

7
successively in the two settings

Setting 1)

for all

j=1\ldots N
,
W_{j}=\mathrm{e}^{\beta\,\xi_{j}}
and

\tilde{Y}_{j}=\xi_{j}
;

Setting 2)

for all

j=1\ldots N
,
W_{j}=\mathrm{e}^{\beta\,\xi_{j}}
and
\tilde{Y}_{j}=1
.

In both settings, we have
F^{\leftarrow}(u)=\beta\,\Phi^{-1}(u)
. Then

Proposition

7
gives us that there exists a
non-decreasing random sequence
\left(I_{N}\right)_{N\geq 1}
such that, for
all
N\geq 1
,
I_{N}
is valued in
\left\{1,\dots,N\right\}
with

W_{I_{N}}=\max\left(W_{1},\dots,W_{N}\right)
and, for all

m,\delta\in[1,\infty)
,
\underline{b}<2<\overline{b}
,

p\in(1,\infty)
,
u>0
and
N\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, we have
in
(i)Setting 1)
and
(i)Setting 2)
,

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}(\overline{W}_{i,N}(\beta))^{\,\delta}\,\tilde{Y}_{i}-\tilde{Y}_{I_{N}}\right\|_{m}

\displaystyle\leq C\,\left(u\,\sup_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle b\in\left[\underline{b},\overline{b}\right]}}{{q=1,m}}}\left(\zeta(b\,\sqrt{\log N})\right)^{\frac{q}{m}}+\tilde{\zeta}_{N}^{(m,p)}\left(u,\left(\log N\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\,N^{1-\frac{\overline{b}}{2}}\right)\right)\;,

where
\zeta
is defined in (
172
),
\tilde{\zeta}_{N}^{(m,p)}
is defined in (
173
) and
C>0
is a constant only depending on
p
,
\underline{b}
,

\overline{b}
,
\delta
and
m
. By

Lemma

7
, using

F^{\leftarrow}
as above, we have that there exists a universal
constant
C_{0}>0
such that, for all
b>0
,

\displaystyle\beta\geq 2b\sqrt{\log N}\geq 1\Longrightarrow\zeta(b\,\sqrt{\log N})\leq C_{0}\penalty\ \frac{b\,\sqrt{\log N}}{\beta}\;.

Note that if
b\leq\overline{b}
and
\beta\geq 2\,(\overline{b}\vee C_{0})\sqrt{\log N}\geq 1
, then the condition on the left-hand side is
satified and the upper bound in the right-hand side is at most 1.
Using this in the previous display for the special case
p=2
, we get that for all

m,\delta\in[1,\infty)
and
\overline{b}>2
, there exists

C,C_{0}>0
such that, for all
u>0
, if
\beta\geq 2\,(\overline{b}\vee C_{0})\sqrt{\log N}\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}(\overline{W}_{i,N}(\beta))^{\,\delta}\,\tilde{Y}_{i}-\tilde{Y}_{I_{N}}\right\|_{m}

\displaystyle\leq C\,\left(u\left(\frac{\overline{b}\,C_{0}\sqrt{\log N}}{\beta}\right)^{\frac{1}{m}}+\tilde{\zeta}_{N}^{(m,2)}\left(u,\left(\log N\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\,N^{1-\frac{\overline{b}}{2}}\right)\right)\;.

(204)

To prove (
202
) and (
203
) we now consider
(i)Setting 1)
and
(i)Setting 2)
separately
by taking
\tilde{Y}_{j}=\xi_{j}
and
\tilde{Y}_{j}=1
respectively in
\tilde{\zeta}_{N}^{(m,2)}\left(u,\left(\log N\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\,N^{-1-\frac{\overline{b}}{2}}\right)
and choosing
adequate values
of
\overline{b}
and
u
in the upper bound above. Namely, we will show that

•

If
\tilde{Y}_{j}=\xi_{j}
for all
j\geq 1
, then: as
N\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\tilde{\zeta}_{N}^{(m,2)}\left(\sqrt{10\log N},\left(\log N\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\,N^{-2}\right)=o\left(\log N\right)\;.

(205)

•

If
\tilde{Y}_{j}=1
for all
j\geq 1
, then: for all
N\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\tilde{\zeta}_{N}^{(m,2)}\left(2,\left(\log N\right)^{1-\frac{\overline{b}}{2}}\right)=(\log N)^{-\frac{1}{4}}\,N^{\frac{3}{2}-\frac{\overline{b}}{4}}\;.

(206)

Observe then that (
206
) follows directly from plugging in
(u,s)=\left(2,\left(\log N\right)^{1-\frac{\overline{b}}{2}}\right)
in (
173
). As for (
205
): setting
\tilde{Y}_{j}=\xi_{j}
for all
j\geq 1
and plugging in
(u,s)=(\sqrt{10\log N},\left(\log N\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\,N^{-2})
in (
173
) yields that for all

N\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\tilde{\zeta}_{N}^{(m,2)}\left(\sqrt{10\log N},\left(\log N\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\,N^{-2}\right)

\displaystyle=\left\|\xi_{1}\right\|_{p\,m}^{1/m}(\log N)^{-\frac{1}{4}}+N\left\|\xi_{1}\,\mathbbm{1}_{\left\{\xi_{N}^{*}>\sqrt{10\log N}\right\}}\right\|_{m}\;,

where

\xi_{N}^{*}=\max\left(\left|\xi_{1}\right|,\dots,\left|\xi_{N}\right|\right)
. Since

\left\|\xi_{1}\,\mathbbm{1}_{\left\{\xi_{N}^{*}>\sqrt{10\log N}\right\}}\right\|_{m}\leq\left\|\xi_{1}\right\|_{2m}\left\|\mathbbm{1}_{\left\{\xi_{N}^{*}>\sqrt{2(1+c)\log N}\right\}}\right\|_{2m},

the desired result (
205
) will follow if we can show that: as
N\to\infty
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\xi_{N}^{*}>\sqrt{10\log N}\right)=o\left(N^{m/2}\right)\;.

Writing that
\xi_{N}^{*}=\max(M_{N},M^{\prime}_{N})
where we have set

M_{N}=\max\left(\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{N}\right)
and
M^{\prime}_{N}=\max\left(-\xi_{1},\dots,-\xi_{N}\right)
, it holds that

\mathbb{P}\left(\xi_{N}^{*}>u\right)\leq\mathbb{P}(M_{N}>u)+\mathbb{P}(M^{\prime}_{N}>u)=2\mathbb{P}(M_{N}>u)
for any
u>0
, hence the last
display follows from (
168
) in

Lemma

5
with
c=4
.

Now, for any given
\overline{b}>2
, if
N,\beta\to\infty
with

\sqrt{\log N}=o(\beta)
, we eventually have

\beta\geq 2\,(\overline{b}\vee C_{0})\sqrt{\log N}\geq 1
so
that (
204
) eventually applies. If

\tilde{Y}_{j}=\xi_{j}
for all
j
, we have

\tilde{Y}_{I_{N}}=\max(\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{N})
, and the
bound (
204
) with
\overline{b}=6
among
with (
205
), gives us that, as
N,\beta\to\infty
with
\sqrt{\log N}=o(\beta)
, (
202
) hold.
If now

\tilde{Y}_{j}=1
for all
j
, we have

\tilde{Y}_{I_{N}}=1
and the
bound (
204
) with
\overline{b}
large enough
among with (
206
), gives us that (
203
) holds.

(ii)

Proofs of (
199
)
and (
200
)
. Since
the expectation is 1-Lipschitz for the
L^{1}
-norm, the
asymptotic behavior (
202
) with

m=1
implies that, for any
\delta\geq 1
,

\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}(\overline{W}_{i,N}(\beta))^{\,\delta}\,\xi_{i}\right)=\mathbb{E}\left(\max(\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{N})\right)_{m}+o\left(\sqrt{\log N}\right)\;.

Applying
Lemma

6
with
m=1
the first term in the
right-hand side is asymptotically equivalent to
\sqrt{2\log N}
and
we get (
199
). The cases
\delta=1,2

in particular together give that, for any
\lambda\in[0,1]
,

\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{N}[\lambda\overline{W}_{j,N}(\beta)+(1-\lambda)(\overline{W}_{j,N}(\beta))^{2}]\,\xi_{j}\right)=\left(2\,\log N\right)^{1/2}\left(1+o(1)\right)\;.

(207)

By the Minkowski inequality for the
L^{2}
-norm, we obtain that

\displaystyle\left|\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{N}[\lambda\overline{W}_{j,N}(\beta)+(1-\lambda)(\overline{W}_{j,N}(\beta))^{2}]\,\xi_{j}\right\|_{2}-\left\|\max(\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{N})\right\|_{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{N}[\lambda\overline{W}_{j,N}(\beta)+(1-\lambda)(\overline{W}_{j,N}(\beta))^{2}]\,\xi_{j}-\max(\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{N})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{N}\overline{W}_{j,N}(\beta)\,\xi_{j}-\max(\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{N})\right\|_{2}+(1-\lambda)\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{N}(\overline{W}_{j,N}(\beta))^{2}\,\xi_{j}-\max(\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{N})\right\|_{2}.

Hence, using (
202
)
again, this time with
m=2
, we deduce

\displaystyle\left|\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{N}[\lambda\overline{W}_{j,N}(\beta)+(1-\lambda)(\overline{W}_{j,N}(\beta))^{2}]\,\xi_{j}\right\|_{2}-\left\|\max(\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{N})\right\|_{2}\right|=o\left(\sqrt{\log N}\right),

which we can rewrite equivalently as

\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{N}[\lambda\overline{W}_{j,N}(\beta)+(1-\lambda)(\overline{W}_{j,N}(\beta))^{2}]\,\xi_{j}\right\|_{2}=\left\|\max(\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{N})\right\|_{2}+o\left(\sqrt{\log N}\right).

Using
Lemma

6
with
m=2
to evaluate the first term in
the right-hand side and taking the sqaure, we get that

\mathbb{E}\left(\left(\sum_{j=1}^{N}[\lambda\overline{W}_{j,N}(\beta)+(1-\lambda)(\overline{W}_{j,N}(\beta))^{2}]\,\xi_{j}\right)^{2}\right)=2\,\log N\,(1+o(1))\;.

This asymptotic behavior with the square
of (
207
) yields (
200
).

(iii)

Proof of (
201
)
. The
proof of (
201
) is obtained similarly to the proofs of (
199
)
and (
